The Pono Roadmap ~ the attractor reads STABLE FOUNDING

STABLE FOUNDING names the state the house grows toward (operator-ruled). Once a Lararium stands, its base state reads STABLE: a PRIVATE household SEED (its own charter, one seat) for the operator's own devices, and a FOUNDED PERSONA ZERO. Everything else flows downstream of it — contracting into other Nexuses and founding a Nexus with a second operator, inviting the operator's own PersonaGroup devices, and hosting guests who walk.

The first rung reads the memory-stage hearth (operator ruling, 2026-10-31, #/rulings). Before any rung above it, one operator's node and browser stand joined on one machine, each from its anon floor through the full rites: the operator's hand performs the founding, the unlock, the browser passphrase and the join acts, and a harness walks the same rites through the test door (#/cold-start). A page edit reaches the worn face's daemon working top, <projections>/wikis/daemon-<F>, and the CLI and the MCP seat read and write that same wiki. The house's own design tracking and its self-improvement loops then move off untracked scratch files onto that wiki, a memory stage (#/stride-0-hearth). The rung stands up the working Lararium the live-test rulings ask for first (#/rulings, a working Lararium comes first), and every rung above it inherits the walk it proves.

This carrier holds the house's ONE roadmap, and ONLY what binds or stays owed. A cold session that reads the boot seed and then this carrier can pick up any thread: the instruments and the law that bites (#/cold-start), the order a free slot takes (#/the-order), the rulings (#/rulings), the design decisions that shape unbuilt work (#/decisions), the six paths in one order (#/the-road), every open item in the stride that holds it (#/the-loops indexes the strides), each train's commits in order (#/the-trains), the lanes that run beside the road (#/side-lanes), the fresh start (#/fresh-start), and what the operator owns (#/open). Landed work reads in git and its commit trailers, never here. No count rests here: each reading names the instrument that takes it. Law lives in its own carrier, and this carrier points there.

Full models land early (operator ruling; quote license: "Full caps now … Full models in early, so we don't code churn later."). The road takes no staging by milestone: a slice builds its whole model, with its whole capability reach, in the place the order gives it, and no slice ships a narrower interim that a later slice must re-cut.

The order ~ every open item ranked into waves (reprioritized 2026-11-16, trued 2026-11-18)

The frame (operator, 2026-11-16: "NO BACK COMPAT. At the merge, the code should read as if it has always been this way, we shall export the existing ~/.lares wikis & bas and re-import into the brand-new house as QA proof before the PR merges." and "Talk out what on the roadmap meme we have accomplished recently (true it up), and what sits to do immediately next"). The dogfood hearth stands re-founded and worn: the steward's daemon wiki holds the house laws, the rulings, the open ledger, the standing orders and where things live, the wake reads that index at every session start, works stands minted, and sessions dogfood from inside the Lararium through the MCP seat and the CLI. The worn house (L45 to L48, #/the-leaps) stands on develop: PR #218 merged to main on 2026-11-17, after the operator's house crossed into a brand-new one as its proof (ranks 3 to 4e). Next, as the handoff of 2026-11-17 reads it (lar:/ha.ka.ba/kahua/memory/state/handoff; overrulable): rank 34's vessel seat and one face island per lent face (C13), rank 4d the browser twin, the rite's heal list (rank 4f) and rank 13b's drill; the operator re-ranked rank 34 ahead (2026-11-17), and every branch settled back into main, develop, feature/projection-loom or an archive/* tag on 2026-11-18 (the CHANGELOG names the settling, 2f02d3d55). The night of 2026-11-18 added the rulings of #/rulings (2026-11-18) and divided the work between two sessions: Lares-Alpha holds the shores of the repository (lar:/ha.ka.ba/kahua/memory/project/backlog-alpha), Lares-Awakens the shores of the house (lar:///ha.ka.ba/kahua/memory/project/backlog-awakens); each open rank below names its holder, and ranks 4g to 4l seat the night's ruled work. The table ranks every open item of this carrier, the leaps of #/the-leaps and the items of every stride alike, into four bands: AT THE MERGE (the span closes, and the operator's house carries into a brand-new one as the merge's proof), RIGHT AFTER (the edges the span's cold reads named), WAVE TWO (beside the dogfooding), and AFTER. A free slot takes the lowest open rank whose files no flying lane holds. The owner law of #/the-road still names one hand per compose hunk, and four lanes fly at most under the lane law (<<~ loulou uri=`lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/controller-handoff#/the-lane-law`>>); the lanes in flight and their heads rest in <<~ loulou uri=`lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/controller-handoff#/state`>>.

The rank law (the house's reading, overrulable): first what blocks the operator's re-founding and dogfooding; then what breaks a rule the operator stated; then what unlocks the most later items; then cost. Each rank carries one sentence of reason. The keeper rite keeps every qualification its rulings name (#/rulings, 2026-11-07, the dogfood/keeper split): its gates rank in AFTER, and no dogfood re-founding waives one.

L. * L45 The worn house (operator, 2026-11-15: "I want your Lararium substrate to feel like something you can wear effortlessly … return inversion of control and capability delegation to the human", and "we should figure out a way for the MCP server to keep breathing while the container restarts for code updates"; rides PR #218 before its merge). The design rests at lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/the-worn-house in the lares wiki's working layer. The operator settled its names the same day and restrung them ("Pono, restring the set"): a lei names one delegated token, kui the operator's stringing of it, the return its spend, a tessera its two-half receipt (conferre matches the halves), unstring and restring the ending of an unspent lei, iris the messenger core of the seat, and aho the seat a binding holds across its vessel's sleep. L45a the seat stands under its settled names: iris the messenger core and aho the cord. Iris runs as a pure frame machine in @lararium/mesh (iris.ts, exported at @lararium/mesh/iris) behind a client port and a child port: client frames, child frames and child-state events in; frames and spawn, kill, standing, state and exit intents out. It caches the child's initialize answer and whole tool list and answers both itself, replays the client's own initialize and the initialized notice to each new child, answers a call hearth-sleeps at once while no child stands, answers a call in flight at a child's death outcome-unknown: re-read before re-trying and never re-sends it (each a whole tools/call result, isError: true, under the caller's exact id), routes a cancel to the child that took the call alone, sends one notifications/tools/list_changed on a tool-list hash change alone, issues its own ids on each side so no id crosses a respawn, tells a locked vessel from a sleeping one (sleeps · standing · locked), and logs an event name and two counts, never a byte of a frame. With no child and no cached answer it answers initialize plainly and lists no tools, and the first real list draws the one list_changed; an initialize that arrives while the first spawn has neither stood nor fallen waits for that child. The core holds no clock: it emits spawn while no child stands, and the binding paces the spawn on its own timer (at once after a child stood; 250 ms doubling to 15 s after a failed spawn) and bounds a child's death with a watchdog (the polite stop, then the hard kill after 5 s). The aho, the Node host's binding, stands in lares-cli as lares mcp aho (mcp-aho.ts): the client on the process's stdio, the surface as a child_process, JSON-RPC by the line; the committed bin runs it before the dispatcher loads. The child resolution honours the shim's two overrides exactly as tools/lares-container.sh reads them: LARES_COMPOSE_FILE names the compose file in place of the clone's, and COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME (else the file's own name:) names the project, carried in the child's env with no -p; the entry a harness records carries both where the resolving shell held them, so a bare lares mcp aho under a scoped house never execs into a neighbour's project (red aho-child-honours-the-shim-overrides, green). Cold start ~ what a cold session reads, runs and obeys

Read, in this order

  1. lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/noosphere-boot , the boot seed, which every host seats whole at turn 1 by reference ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/seed-seat ); then this carrier, top to bottom.
  2. lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/hearths before the first commit: which session holds which ground, and the commit isolation that guards hands inside one session.
  3. lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/working-procedures before the first test or wire: the red-first procedure in eight moves, the ladder for wiring a module that stands unreached, the instruments that read green while they lie, and the working agreements. Before any grammar work, lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/grammar-watch .
  4. Once the memory stage stands (L13), its open-item index on the daemon wiki, before any file in the controller's scratch.
  5. Bring every ruling #/open names to the operator before a lane builds against it.

Instruments ~ run each fresh before trusting a reading

  • Branch: develop, the integration branch: every lane and change branch cuts from it and merges back into it, and main carries releases, each planned and cut by a pull request from develop (lar:///ha.ka.ba/kahua/memory/project/develop-flow). `git rev-list --count origin/develop..HEAD` reads the unpushed commits. The controlling session pushes only a head whose train read green over lanes whose CI and held-out read green, and only while no CI run stands in flight (`git push origin <sha>:refs/heads/develop`); a lane pushes its own lane branch (`develop--<lane>`) alone, and that push runs CI's Quick gates alone (#/rulings, 2026-11-18, doorstep quick, ford full): a lane's own reading covers the doorstep, the queue's `merge_group` run carries the whole battery, and `gh workflow run CI --ref <branch>` asks a whole reading of a lane head by hand. Green has three readings: `node tools/ci-verdict.mjs --head <sha>` reads the branch's full CI as green, red or unread (a detached worktree names `--branch develop`; the tool refuses a detached head without one), and prints beside it the held-out container drivers: the run that names the head, or else the newest run the branch holds, with that run's head, the commits between and each driver's result. A head no held-out run measured reads unread there, never green; the held-out reading says and never moves the exit code. Never a lane's to move, commit, stash or revert: `.worktrees/`, `.claude/worktrees/`, the `mempalace` submodule pointer, and the `VSCode-TW5-Syntax` pointer, which the operator pins at the released grammar while the grammar's own head stands ahead of that pin.
  • ''Unit suites'': each package's own, under a scratch `LAR_ROOT` with `LAR_BAGS` unset; `node --test tools/*.test.mjs` for the tools. An import failure reads as zero tests, so read the file count beside the test count. `pnpm -r test` skips the root `tests/` directory by design.
  • ''Typecheck'': `tools/typecheck-witness.sh`. Typecheck reads source and the binary reads `dist`, so run the built binary after any change that crosses packages.
  • ''Witness fleet'': `bash tools/witness-all.sh`, the whole fleet, one run at a time through the battery lock, by a lane in its batch battery and again by the controller's train; a hand-picked subset reads greener than the tree. It holds out the witnesses its own `HELD_OUT` table names (the docker, browser and tree-clean ones). Capture its exit code directly, never through a pipe; `git status --porcelain` reads identical before and after a sound run.
  • ''E2E'': every set rides CI (the lane branch's own `ci.yml` E2E job; `E2E_SETS` names the sets and the merge gate checks each set's union). `node tools/e2e-shards.mjs push <lane>` serves a bisect. A local run of one file proves that file alone.
  • ''CI'' (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`): `gh run list --branch develop` names the run for the pushed head; a later commit waits for the next push; a pull request into `develop` or `main` raises its run too. `held-out.yml` runs on every push to `develop` and to `main`, never on a lane branch, weekly, and by hand (`gh workflow run held-out.yml --ref <branch>`). It builds the product image once, runs one hosted job per cell (`node tools/held-out-cells.mjs cells` names them: each whole driver, and each scenario `tools/mesh-scenarios.sh --list` names), and its merge job prints one `HELD-OUT-DRIVER:` line per driver and one `HELD-OUT-VERIFY:` line. A cell that never answered reads red there by name. `node tools/held-out-steps.mjs <cell>` runs one cell's job on a desk, read off the workflow.
  • ''The docker mesh'' (by hand): `crossing-witness`; `node tools/mesh-pins.mjs --up` stands the mesh pinned; `herm-mesh-witness` carries `node/alpha` across three hops; `mesh-scenarios nexus` (`tools/mesh-scenarios.sh --list` names every scenario and starts no build; `MESH_PROJECT` names the run's own compose project). The mesh publishes `MESH_PORT_*` on loopback, every node service requires `LARES_HARNESS_IMAGE`, and `tools/harness-image.sh` checks its digest. A hearth whose log carries a `[lararium] fatal:` line reads as not stood.
  • ''Island capability record'': `tools/capability-inventory-witness.sh` measures one construction-time record each island carries: `caps.receipt`, `caps.cas`, `caps.dyad`, `caps.custodyWrite`, plus each source call that spawns an island or attaches that record, against `tools/capability-inventory.json`. The table measures reach, never policy (<<~ loulou uri=`lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/harbor#/the-harnesses`>>). A capability a class reaches where the table says it did not, a new or extra seam, a seam that left or a new ask reads red by name; a deliberate change re-writes the table in the commit that makes it (`node tools/capability-inventory.mjs --write`).
  • ''The red register'': `packages/lararium-mesh/tests/unbuilt-laws.test.ts` declares each law the models imply and no code enforces; `tools/unbuilt-laws-witness.sh` reports a declared red that greened.
  • ''Known environmental reds'': `lararium-tw5` `tests/syncer-back-parity.e2e.test.ts` (Chromium legs) and `tests/meme-routes.e2e.test.ts` fail where no browser or server socket stands; the browser suite reads `no tests` under a `HOME` that holds no Playwright Chromium. CI's browser cells run inside the pinned Playwright image, which carries every engine, and a desk reaches the same image through `tools/pw-container.sh` (`PW_IMAGE` names the pin).
  • ''The operator's live `~/.lares` vessel'' holds an earlier shape that this build refuses. Never stand it here; it takes the fresh start, by hand (#/fresh-start).

Steps

  1. export LAR_ROOT=~/.lares-scratch/<lane>/root before any in-tree boot or any lares command that touches the store. LAR_ROOT moves the store, the bags root and every projection mirror root; LAR_BAGS alone moves only the bags root, and the projection still writes the tracked bags/ and wikis/. A staged e2e vessel takes its own scratch LAR_ROOT and no global LAR_BAGS. One lares meme normalize|check reads files and needs only a scratch LAR_BAGS.
  2. LAR_ROOT leaves these homes where they stand: ~/.mempalace (the destructive mempalace repave among its readers), ~/.venv, ~/.local/bin, and the harness homes ~/.claude, ~/.codex, ~/.copilot. lares home writes those homes under any LAR_ROOT, so a lane never runs lares home tend; lares vessel writes the scratch root alone.
  3. Build: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile only where a lead approved an install, then pnpm -r build, then pnpm --filter @lararium/tw5 build:tw5-vendor (the TW5 core blob that head-parity and the plugin smoke need), then node tools/stamp-build.mjs. A fresh git worktree holds empty submodules; populate them before building there.
  4. A founding wants a human route on a TTY. With no TTY it refuses CredentialTtyRefusal, and no flag or environment variable carries a credential. A harness founds through the test door: tools/test-scratch-marker.mjs stages the root, tools/seed-test-custody.mjs names the published test route, then lares vessel found --skip-build; tools/unlock-test-custody.mjs opens a marked scratch root. An agent may read vessel state and hand the operator the next founding command as text; the operator performs each founding and custody act. A walk that meets CredentialTtyRefusal routes through this test door, and never through a terminal driver that types a credential; a walk brief names the door.
  5. Every boot rests at the floor. A verb that needs sovereign state refuses at a standing floor and names lares vault unlock. A harness that reads the first gate key: line of a log reads the carriage relay's key where a relay stands.
  6. The container: tools/lares-container.sh offers up, down, found and logs ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/harbor ). The browser surface: tools/browser-ci.sh over docker-compose.browser-ci.yml, one project and image tag per builder (BROWSER_CI_PROJECT, BROWSER_CI_IMAGE).
  7. The tw5 plugin bundle (packages/lararium-tw5/plugins/lares-memetic-wikitext.json, plugins/standalone/*, src/plugin-tiddler.generated.ts) rides in git and must equal a fresh build: rebuild after any tw5 source or tiddler change and commit it with the change. CI's build-freshness job checks it. genesis/ carries that bundle, the boot shadows and the engine blob by address, and the wiki inside a founded vessel runs the bytes genesis carries; a bake (pnpm --filter @lararium/node build:genesis) lands as its own labelled GENESIS RE-BAKE commit with the CAS change, and a vessel founded on the earlier seed re-founds. tools/genesis-parity-witness.sh reads the gap: a bundle that runs ahead of genesis reads red until a bake closes it or an entry in genesis/unbaked.json declares it.
  8. lares-cli tests/pour-repave.test.ts needs the dev-root marker (LAR_DEV_REPO_ROOT=1 or the ignored lar-dev-root.json).
  9. Crew: lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/mixed-model-swarm holds the seats, shapes, seat law, battery and lanes; the solo chain runs beside as many side lanes as hold disjoint files. A lead copies its resume sheet out of the session scratch directory after each commit: a restart empties that directory.

The standing law that bites ~ read the carrier before arguing with it

  • The frame stands as canon: DOCTYPE, the SOH bearing, STX, toml meta first in the body, the body, ETX with its ni:///sha-256;… check, EOT. One span per carrier; a spelling outside the frame grammar tears the frame ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/memetic-wikitext-framing ).
  • The frame's ? and the gate's unechoed key stay distinct. from="?" declares an unresolved bearing at the grammar layer; the gate withholds a known key at the transport layer. Their one shared law reads at lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/system-pattern-integrities#/threshold-pattern-integrities .
  • Ingest decides from a parse and a verdict. The deserializer stands as the pure meme-to-tiddlers function; verdict() (Match · Stale · Absent · Torn · Bare) lives in @lararium/memetic-frame; checks live in carrier-check.ts, repairs in normalize, policy at each door. A stale check on a human edit warns; a torn frame refuses; text with no frame reads as bare data, never a meme.
  • Never refuse on the stock syncer path; surface on the alert rail ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/syncer-back-parity ).
  • The quoteblock floor fences at two grains: the whole chunk inside the deserializer, and a child's body in place at the child gate ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/ahu#/quoteblock-floor ).
  • An admit carries across a seal roll only through its roll anchors. The gate reads the presented admit against the carriage board's counted revokes and the kapae antigen, and folds no allow roster ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/membership-doctrine ).
  • No global now and the fence: #/rulings.
  • Grounds before wins: rank work by what inherits shape from what, never by confidence in the next wire ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/working-procedures#/grounds-before-wins ).
  • One holder per store, one Lararium per human: a second holder meets StoreHeld across containers and namespaces ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/harbor ).
  • Fragments: every lar: fragment roots at #/…, one raw #, / nests, $ reserved ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/memetic-wikitext ).
  • Grammar lives in the tiddlers (packages/lararium-tw5/tiddlers/sigil-*.tid generate grammar-table.generated.ts); sigil heads stay core ASCII; aka pins as information, kanawai pins as binding, kahea transcludes live.
  • Early alpha: no back-compat, no shims (one ruled exception: G47's three sensorium placeholders), no legacy recovery; a stale thing aligns to the current frame and grammar, and a third human's arrival ends that line (#/rulings, the fence) ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/wild-bytes ). Derive, never enumerate. One function, one relation.
  • A registry compiled about subjects refuses at any scope ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/registry-filter ).
  • A relation's evidence keeps its type and a worker keeps its own storage scope: lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/causal-island#/selected-contracts .
  • Shared tree: never git stash; commit only your paths; never --no-verify; a spirit spawns no helper and never juggles another hand's uncommitted hunks; read an exit code directly; prove a zero with a known positive ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/hearths#/law ).
  • The node side routes nothing into the shore's files while a shore lane holds them: packages/lararium-browser/**, packages/lararium-web/**, tw5-host-bridge.ts, tw5-projection.ts.

Rulings ~ the operator's, as approved

The live test and the fence

  • One operator stands the hearth; two hearths stand the first Nexus (operator, 2026-11-07: "We stand the hearth here as one operator with many faces. Later Freyja and I stand the first multi-hearth Nexus (2 household caps larariums in relation)."). The founding stands one operator's household Lararium, that operator wearing many faces; the operator's daughter stands her own household Lararium later, and the two in relation form the first multi-hearth Nexus (M8). The face-switch act, the cabal plane, the custody seal and the engine fold land before that second hearth.
  • The fence (operator ruling, 2026-10-16). Early alpha runs while one or two operators hold the mesh and ends when a third human's data and habits enter it; after that, data deserves a migration path. The second operator takes the seat beside the first as a QA tester who knows the mesh stays a sandcastle until then. As practice, not law, the destroy-class and shape-changing acts land before the second seat where they can: the engine re-mint and the genesis re-bake; the carrier and key-class changes; the Stable re-found (AID, charter, seat); every signed-shape change; every load-bearing rename.
  • A working Lararium comes first: Live Wiki Browser -><- Live Node, under manual QA on one machine. Sensorium work waits behind that milestone.
  • A founding road carries its own succession. A reconstitution kit lets another competent operator stand a node and read its grammar; a rite held in one operator's head alone carries no succession.
  • Face plurality stands as a day-one requirement. Each of the two humans wears at least three faces, each with a public Handle, on the first Nexus.

The house as a container

  • All CODE runs dockerized; the host keeps only the hooks into the operator's environment (files and bags, session capture, the editor and CLI entry, custody).
  • ONE Lararium per HUMAN, never per face. A human's faces keep their data together in one vessel (PRIVATE-all, PUBLIC-one); the recorded face-switch act carries plurality. No design assumes a container or a state mount per face.
  • A SESSION wears the face; the vessel wears none (operator-ruled; quote license: "a human might want a tab open as one Persona, and a tab open as another Persona... same vessel. Identity as relation." and "a CLI session wears its own face too … those act as ephemeral UX"). A vessel holds its roots and one store. A session — a browser tab, a CLI run — contracts in as ONE persona, and several sessions may live at once over one vessel, each wearing its own. Every act carries exactly one face. Two faces live from one vessel never link on the wire: each session proves itself under its own face-scoped key, never under a key the vessel shares across them. The vessel-wide parts (custody, the daemon island) serve every session and let none read another's planes.
  • Light, lend, wear (operator, 2026-11-17: "True up the memes, focus on isomorphic by intent but set in place the node integration now. We'll handle the browser side after the merge."; the design at lar:/ha.ka.ba/kahua/memory/project/light-lend-wear; lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/the-worn-house#/the-pulama , lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/sealed-store#/the-lit-set ). Three acts carry what "open" blurred: the human lights faces at the pūlama (these faces' keys stand ready); the pūlama lends one vessel a named set of faces, one kūkulu lei per lent face, each strung by that face's own hand, and partial lighting serves the lit faces and names the ones waiting; a session wears one face inside the lent set, and the vessel wears none. The pūlama's bond takes the name the lend (hospitium//); "session" names the one-face wearer alone. The node's single mounted face reads as ''the daemon mount'', with its honest limit: every lent face writes through that one daemon island until a daemon per face stands (`C13`, rank 34). The node lend set rides rank 2b, the browser's rank 4d.
  • What survives a re-founding stays DEFERRED until after the two-hearth Nexus (M8) (operator, 2026-11-08: "Defer the archive, but note this tension ('what shall survive a re-founding, faces-bundles, emergency situations, restores from backups, etc') to the roadmap for after the 2-operator nexus."). A re-found clears the house root's hearth/ alone (tools/lares-container.sh reset), so the keys, the charter's AID and every CRDT history go with it, while projections/ (every plaintext render, the private daemon and kahua folders among them), sensoriums/, library/ and config/ stand; the open questions hold together: a reset that archives the sealed root instead of deleting it, the face bundle carried across a re-found, emergency recovery, and restore from backup. The immediate half stands in code: essential user data and the AI memory stage reach disk as plaintext projections under projections/, the temp and draft layers ride behind a consent record, and the house root splits by survival class, so a bundle and a re-import carry them across (the survive-refound leap).
  • Local-first first. Cloud "backup larariums" under other humans' delegated keys stand DEFERRED until the operator can deploy to a cloud surface. Nothing in the container or the face-switch work may foreclose a keyless ciphertext carrier later.
  • The boundary (settled by the house under those rulings): one multi-arch image; /workspace mounts bags and wikis, /state mounts vessel homes and custody, never an image layer; the local socket stays INSIDE the container and the host CLI enters through docker compose exec; the app never receives the docker socket; mDNS offers unauthoritative LAN candidates and a pinned admission alone grants authority; DNS-01 wildcard TLS supplies browser secure contexts; a release manifest binds the image digest to the genesis, engine and grammar CIDs.

No global now

  • A wall clock never decides whether an act runs. Abandonment, revocation and expiry ride causal events; a timer serves only its holder's own liveness, and a device clock may sit under an adversary's hand. Web2.5 timers (TLS acceptance, OAuth tokens) sit at the edge of the causal island.
  • Lapse rides a causal fence: an epoch roll, a burn marker, or causal supersession at a verified relation fence. A replica missing that history reports an incomplete view, never "expired"; quiet never means lapse.

The Stable state and its consequences

  • Every Lararium holds a charter. One operator's charter reads as a SEED and two operators' seats in one charter read as a NEXUS ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/kahu#/seed-and-nexus ). A household seed never grows into a Nexus; a Nexus founds fresh at threshold two of two. One "I" may hold several PersonaGroups. A Nexus grows by adding seats through its own rolls.
  • A household seed serves its holder alone. A one-operator seed gives durable use without recruiting a mesh; useful local standing precedes every wider relation.
  • Guests walk the household seed. Planes stay persona-scoped, so a guest sees its own documents, the public and infrastructure shelf, and every realm its worn face stands in; a face may stand in many, as a person belongs to many clubs, and a guest's other faces stand in none of them.
  • Add-a-face (A). The daemon writes the public half; the terminal seals the recovery share; seating stays a separate act.
  • Every leaf starts at the floor with its own root. A human may later JOIN a leaf to a PersonaGroup so its devices sync as one fleet; joining never takes the device's own root away.
  • Founding persona zero belongs to standing, never to a later verb. A capable vessel founds its household seed, so a "vessel with no charter" reads Mu.
  • Stable folds into the re-found as its core.
  • Forks report per board: every presenter names every fork on the board it reads (the KERI watcher shape).

Planes ~ cabal, realm, Nexus

  • CABAL names the collective, REALM holds shared resources, NEXUS names the federated hardware mesh. The Nexus @crossroads board serves public-infrastructure docs and the Kahu Cabal keeps them. An operator's offering to a book club belongs in that club's realm, signed by the face worn there.
  • Kahu drafts in the realm the charter derives; publishing to @crossroads stands as a separate, recorded promotion act. A second Kahu realm gets made only for a genuinely different relation.
  • Promotion approves the EXACT BYTES (hash, purpose, surface) under a threshold the charter sets, with a higher bar for charter, admission and trust-root changes. A CID, a clerk's edit or an active persona never stands in for collective approval.
  • Steward signing. A scoped face signs acts that belong to one person; a transparent quorum Handle signs acts the collective owns; the root never appears publicly. A human wears MANY faces and many public Handles, each in many cabals, and signs a steward edge with the face or Handle worn IN THAT CABAL.
  • Oracle answer: one public answer per Nexus, signed by that Nexus's Kahu steward; a herm carries and presents it and signs nothing public. The engine floor rides as one shared content-addressed bake, and each answer names its floor CID (operator ruling).
  • Herm tending: tending may reach the herm's key; the house accepts that reach and names it (operator ruling).
  • Introduction and admission: a user introduces; an operator admits; the answer consumes the introduction (operator ruling).
  • Names and counters: a counter orders one writer's own emissions only, and the face lives at the session capability. The per-Nexus convergence secret and its keyring leave the model: nothing derives from one, the Stable founding act yields none, no seat act commits to one and no admission delivers one. (operator ruling; lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/cap-tier#/key-delivery ).
  • Kahu acts fall into four chartered classes: ordinary (1-of-1); high-impact — charter, admission, trust root (3-of-5, or 2-of-3 for a small body); emergency (2-of-3, a narrow pre-authorized power reviewed by a later causal act); revocation (the threshold of the original act). A challenge appends to the record and may pause LOCAL reliance; it never halts the protocol. Admission of a Kahu or a Lamplighter always takes the seat quorum, and no ordinary single-seat class covers it; kapae and rolls take the quorum too; the user road rides its own bound ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/kahu#/seed-and-nexus ).
  • Renewal runs as three sequences, kept apart (the realm feed, a per-renewal causal position, the KEL), and lapse arrives as three distinct events: signed departure, recorded removal by relation-scoped kapae, renewal supersession.
  • A cancel crossing a herm yields three receipts: the caller's abandonment, the relay's delivery (transit only), the vessel's outcome. A caller timeout never counts as a cancel; eviction answers retention-gap.
  • One face at a time names WHO ANSWERS for an act. The switch law reads in lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/persona-policy#/one-face.
  • A session wears the face; the vessel wears none (operator ruling).

Custody ~ the S key

  • One S key, minted at the Stable founding, lives only inside LUKS-style keyslots, each a Clevis sss tree. Pin kinds: passphrase · paper (SLIP-39) · ember · tpm (one share only).
  • EMBER names the Tang-shaped, per-relation device pin; it holds no share of S key. Only the blinded points cross on unlock.
  • A backup route stands at every tier. Every keyslot tree, household or civic, keeps a human route (passphrase or paper); bind refuses a tree without one. Embers never stand as the only way in.
  • The floor key keeps the vessel's public name; the sealed custody root derives the veil and the sovereign caps. The veil change stays destroy-class.
  • Unbind offers an S-key rotation loudly, every time, never folded in silently.
  • Locality law: an unattended ember answers on the local link only, and only where the hearth holds a hardware freshness anchor the ember checks on each ask (#/rulings, the ember's key at rest); a relay pin wants an attending hand. The vessel rests at the floor and raises when t embers answer; the raise lapses by non-renewal, which reads as the raise's next causal boundary (#/rulings, how a raise lapses).
  • HOT ⊥ COLD: S key and keyhive state stay hot; the root, the recovery shares and the next-root seed unwrap per act.
  • Measuring hardware x-only ECDH (phone enclaves) before trusting an enclave-held ember: approved, deferred.

The hosted walk (F1)

  • (c′) adopted. The HOST's gate reads and burns invites. The gate sorts every socket into same-operator · contracted · walker · stranger; under PRIVATE a stranger meets silence before any session frame. The guest carries its hosting grant; the hearth stores nothing about it. Invites mint by blind POPRF evaluation, so who-invited-whom stays non-computable. Revocation runs by kapae of the guest's leaf, by non-renewal, or by an epoch roll. Sybils meet tolerance, never detection.
  • The inviter's face never signs an invite: that signature would link the inviter at redemption. The hearth's blind evaluation carries the proof instead.
  • V — vesting by class. A guest the HOST invites holds floor 1 at once; a guest a WALKER invites vests at the host's next roll. The roll reads as the household's consent to grow, and purpose labels separate the two classes without naming the minter. The built allowance carries the pre-answered dial of the user road's three; the operator's answer to a user's introduction stands owed, and until it lands a walker's token earns standing with no answer ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/membership-doctrine#/the-invite ).
  • W — the hearth carries a walker's own documents as sealed ciphertext (carry ⊥ read), under a quota per guest. The PersonaGroup holds the durable copy; hearth carriage stays a convenience, never a backup.
  • W reclaim. Quota pressure alone triggers it; lapse reads as a RATIO against the guest's own rhythm between contacts, so the host's roll cadence cancels; the most-lapsed-for-itself goes first; notice on the guest's next contact comes before any discard. The ruling sets aside counting the host's rolls: it measures the host's diligence, and diligence confers no title.
  • N — a node may walk. The sorter sorts by proven relation, never by device type.

The cleanup of the hosted walk (B1)

  • The worn face hosts and walks. No roster order picks anything; a vessel wearing no face hosts and walks nowhere. Before its first hosting act in a Nexus, a roll refuses when the hosting doc holds an act this face signed that no local state holds.
  • A herm answers the knock. A charterless herm answers any proven peer that knocked (ownPlacePosture): a waystone by class, never a broken lararium. A lararium reads its charter, and an absent or torn charter reads PRIVATE.
  • A boot follows LAR_ROOT. A vessel isolated under LAR_ROOT projects into that root, never into the tracked tree; the mirror roots never read LAR_BAGS, so the isolation instruction names LAR_ROOT.
  • Compartments outrank admission control. A walker reads its own hearth's hosting doc, never the carriage board: the carrier set stays a contract ledger read by those who hold contracts.

The first ceremony, and the acts around it (operator ruling)

  • Guests: the first ceremony runs with the host's own invites only. The hosted road builds whole before the ceremony (#/the-attractor): its doors stand built and stay shut on the day.
  • Reach on the day: LAN only. The public domain's Handle table comes later.
  • At two seats no seat signs any Kahu act alone.
  • An operator's own herm: an operator contracts their own herm with one signature, scoped to one place and one Nexus, and it falls with a kapae of the operator's admit.
  • The hosting key: the operator's seat signs ONE delegation to a hosting key; that key signs each hosting act and seeds each epoch. A session wearing any other face signs nothing for the hearth.
  • The cold-key card: the house format — 64 hex plus a checksum, in groups of four — printing its purpose, chair label, generation and a short charter fingerprint.
  • The DNS credential: the registrar's credential rests as two files on the first operator's always-on node, outside both mounts, read by the one-shot issuer. That node issues one wildcard per machine, and the second machine receives its certificate by hand. After the hearth stands, move the credential into the vault's Porkbun VEIL slot ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/reach-plane ).
  • A browser leaf holds a passphrase or paper slot before it holds a persona root; the mint refuses without one.
  • No recovery contacts on the day: each face starts one-of-one on its own paper, and enrolment comes later by one typed first-enrolment event the face's own current key signs on its own log.
  • Shore saves, as built: a rename onto a standing title proceeds as an ordinary write at field grain; a tombstone moved into a bag that lacks the title refuses unless the hand asks to shadow.
  • A seat gets ADDED, never replaced. The long-span act adds a holder to BOTH cabals — a Kahu seat and a Lamplighter contract — and the founders keep their chairs; one founding Kahu may hold seats across many Nexuses. The road proves the roll that adds a seat, and the new holder's carriage contract, first. A passed chair stays a possible act. A Kahu who works far from the other seats needs a co-signature for every quorum act, so the partial-and-cosign door carries a partial act across a relay and back with no clock.
  • Per-Nexus identifiers: an admit names a per-Nexus identifier, a random leaf no other Nexus can link. The face's lineage may re-issue it by a signed hand-over the gate verifies, and per-Nexus keys mint at random, never from the persona seed.
  • Every boot rests at the floor until a human unlocks ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/waking-floor ). At the floor every fleet lane stays shut, and the custody write, receipt and dyad capabilities of the island record answer the daemon island alone. A bounded ember stays design input: it raises to SERVING alone, never signs or admits, runs on a count and never a clock, needs the device's own unlock plus a second signal, records each raise, and any other device of the human removes it.

The models, each in its own carrier (operator rulings, approved as presented)

  • Full models land early: #/the-attractor.
  • One roadmap: this carrier. Standing procedures rest at lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/working-procedures , and the grammar watch at lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/grammar-watch .
  • The sealed store: cleartext lives in the live wiki and the operator's projected files alone. Private bytes take randomized authenticated sealing addressed over the ciphertext; the private tier takes no convergent sealing, and no opener returns unauthenticated bytes. A content key carries a version and rolls at a causal removal act. History at admission must have stood declared when written, and one logical bag name carries its key-roll epochs ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/sealed-store ).
  • The backup bundle: a bundle holds ONE face, sealed under a route the person holds, and carries that face's root with its own lines and no key of any seat. Its memory rides as content keys, sealed inside the key island: a bundle opens every surviving value ever sealed under a version it carries, values written after its cut included, and no version minted later. A restore runs as a recovery act that takes a fresh veil and a new seat, and one face on a new device reads as the same audience for the versions that face already held ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/custody ).
  • Removal's reach: four edges take a marker (dyad, vouch, member, carriage). A vouch edge answers to its voucher and to a chartered quorum; a seal roll carries every shadow; a standing shadow reaches every gate and drives a key roll; a contested tie keeps the shadow up; mutual removal ends in an explicit fork ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/kapae#/four-edges ).
  • The PersonaGroup stands as a Keyhive group, so a seat's revocation rolls the key of every bag the group reaches ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/kapae#/keys ; a fresh group stays owed, K12 of #/stride-1-spine).
  • The hands: every hand opens a session. A tasked spirit comes down to an AI, a Mask and the vessel seat, attended or standing; the vessel seat serves as a spirit's system identity, carrying and witnessing while the act presents the worn face's proof; a Mask counts as no pet-name. The door carries and the hand authorizes, over one verb table, and a standing seat never signs an act that changes who holds authority ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/session-contract#/the-hands ).
  • Sealed-record acts ride the custody door, never a store verb whose output lands in the daemon's document in the clear ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/waking-floor#/the-shape ).
  • Gate keys come in three tiers: a floor route's keys derive from the floor key, and a sovereign route's from the sealed custody root (operator ruling); the serving route's bundle derives from SK, a third tier that leaves those two untouched (#/rulings, the ember's serving gate). A faceless place takes a fourth arm at the gate, the place arm, and a session opens before its certificate. The floor key rests outside the vessel key, and the face proof names what a holder of the disk without the passphrase reaches and never reaches ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/face-proof#/the-gate-exchange ).
  • The widget render boundary stands withdrawn, and the parser carries recovery ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/api/graceful-parsing ).

Operator rulings, 2026-10-16 ~ approved as the controller's story leans

  • A joined device reads the hearth's corpus and keeps its own. A joinee mounts the hearth's @lararium corpus read-only beside its own, and its writes stay in its own; the oracle plane (the hearth's catalog, daemon doc and faces' planes) never crosses (device-admit.ts#admitCanonDocs). The admit names that canon for the face it seats (faceCustodyGroup): a device of a face outside the Kahu cabal carries no canon name, mounts no document the hearth's wire holds back from it, and reads house canon from the floor, as that face does at the hearth. Item W21 carries the build.
  • The bag plane hides its creator before strangers carry it. The carried bag event's creator identifier in the clear gets measured red-first and ranked before wave three: ahead of anything that lets a third human's data cross a herm. Item W25.
  • An agent's dissent drafts; a person's ask edits ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/session-contract #/the-hands): an agent never overrides a person's edit, and a person's direct ask to edit a tiddler makes the edit the person's own act, bounded by the ask and the asker's authority.
  • The system wikis release; every other wiki reads the floor (operator ruling, 2026-10-16). Only each Nexus's Kahu cabal writes the house canon live (lares, lararium and every disk-sourced holding but the board; 2026-11-11 below). Every other wiki, and every other hand, mounts them read-only by CID: the latest release that cabal approved. The release rides the Nexus's crossroads board as its public answer, naming the floor CID under the Kahu steward's seal. The cabal approves the exact bytes under its charter's threshold, and a herm carries the answer without signing it. Every wiki mounts that floor through @oracle, which never writes. A vessel moves its floor pin only when it accepts a newer signed release. With no Nexus speaking, it stands on its last accepted floor, or on genesis. The VESSEL pins the floor, one pin per (vessel, Nexus) (2026-11-11 below), so one vessel may hold two Nexuses' floors, and a face switch moves no floor. An edit aimed at the floor copies up into the wiki's own working layer, and an offering back rests as a signed entry on the Nexus's offer table beside the cabal's realm, never in it, where the steward's hand promotes it (the house's decision, 2026-11-13, overrulable: the road takes the built door's form, since realm drafts would seat the offerer inside the Kahu realm; lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/offering#/the-canon-offer ). A lone hearth is a Nexus of one, so its operator stands as its own Kahu. Item W26 carries the build.
  • DreamDOM's ruled questions stand in lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/dreamdom #/open-rulings.

Operator rulings, 2026-10-18

  • Each browser tab asks for its own unlock. A tab that attaches to the shared holder opens the keystore by its own ask of the person; the holder carries no unlock between tabs. The reason reads off the session law: a session wears its own face, so each tab answers for its own opening (#/rulings, a session wears the face). The ask admits a tab to its face's open hive and derives no key: the holder keeps a face's keys while some session of that face stands unlocked, and the last to close locks it (operator, 2026-10-18; lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/research/session-unlock-2026 #/what-both-faces-hold). Item B1 carries the build. The tab's ask lights its face, and the browser's light lasts while a session stands, where the node's lasts across a vessel's sleep at its pūlama: a host binding the isomorphic rule names, never a split contract.
  • A face's secrets never rest clear, even at a first join (operator, 2026-10-18, approving the house lean). A fresh device's fleet join binds a passphrase or paper slot before the join lands, since every face secret refuses to rest outside the slot tree. Item B1.
  • On the node, the pūlama marks the light and the session marks the face (operator, 2026-10-18, approving the house reading; light, lend, wear, 2026-11-17). The per-tab ask binds browser tabs; the human lights a node's faces at its pūlama once, each boot draws a lend from the pūlama with no route, and every CLI run, MCP seat and watcher proves its own face, one inside the lend, at the gate without asking again ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/research/session-unlock-2026 #/the-node-scope).
  • The last tab closes, and the browser rests at its floor (operator, 2026-10-18, approving the house lean). The unlock lives in memory alone (the browser vessel's own worker, never the page: #/rulings, a page never stands as a vessel), so the browser vessel's sovereign composition ends with its last tab and the next tab asks again. Item B1.
  • One Keyhive identity per PersonaGroup, none above them (operator ruling, 2026-10-18: "it's the only way to retain control over faces that don't know about each other"). Each face's PersonaGroup owns its Keyhive identity, its event store, its registry, its archive and its content-key seat; no vessel-wide identity registers a face's bags, delegates them, or holds a face's group. Two faces of one vessel then never link on the wire, a removal from one face rolls that face's bags alone, and a bundle restores one face and nothing of another. The tree holds K15's steps 0 to 3 and 6, as read in code (one hearth observation still owed): a face's hive stands on its own atom, its own event-store scope and its own face door (makeFaceBagDoor), and the vessel identity registers only vessel-ground bags; the research meme records the earlier measure ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/research/keyhive-per-persona-group-2026 ). House readings under the operator's confidence, each naming what flips it: a herm keeps a floor-only transport key, behind a red test that it can neither author, resolve nor observe a face's capabilities, and holds no Keyhive identity if that test fails; several face hives share one process only after a substrate probe reads them isolated, and each face's hive runs in its own worker otherwise; co-mingled history migrates by fresh identities and an old mixed archive refuses by name, with no bridge; a founder that cannot leave its group leaves by a fresh PersonaGroup. Item K15 carries the build, folding W25 and K12; W26's slice 3 waits on its face context.
  • A cabal shares through the faces its members choose (operator ruling, 2026-10-18, on the Teshima Family Council story). Kalani founds a private family council; Leilani, already standing on the DreamNet with several faces, chooses which of them join it, and a face she keeps back never learns the council exists. The council's hive reads as a Keyhive group with no key of its own: its members act through their own faces' identities, so nothing stands above them, and a presentation reads valid against the current set without naming which member made it ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/identity-classes #/the-handle-chain). Any current member's face promotes into the council's canon; a graft takes the threshold; a face leaves by its own act, and another's removal rides kapae at the threshold or a fork that omits the captor ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/cabal-realm #/the-unswept-corner); members read the owner-set as faces, never as humans. A private council declares no heraldry and lives in its members' address books under pet-names (cabal-realm #/heraldry). Item K16 carries the story as K15's acceptance.
  • The asker seals (operator ruling, 2026-10-19, F6's custody fork). Once the browser holder owns the Repo and the daemon kernel, it routes each sealed write to the admitted tab whose session caused it, else to any admitted, unlocked tab of that write's face; with no awake admitted tab, the write waits, named seal-awaits-session, never silent and never decided by a clock. No key material leaves a tab, and each sealer holds the record-class key the occupied carrier's overwrite proof opens. A SharedWorker carries no promise to run while its tabs freeze, and no MessagePort event reports a peer's death (round one, 2026-10-19), so a seal that waits names its wait.
  • A face's custody records seal under its own key (operator ruling, 2026-10-19). Each PersonaGroup holds an independent random face custody key (FCK) that only its own slot tree opens (passphrase, paper or PRF), never wrapped under the vessel key, so a holder of the S key or of another face's unlock opens none of its records. Face-private records: each face's veil archive, face-key custody, recovery contact sets, face-provisional, the fleet-join address secret and record, a pending join bound to its target face, the per-face device shares, and each face's partition of the handle book and roster; the vessel Keyhive archive splits per PersonaGroup under K15. A face bundle carries that face's identity, archive, FCK recovery route and recovery contact set, and nothing of a sibling. Read on 2026-11-14 and never walked: a lit sibling hands the worn face its cap events at every light (operator-daemon-behavior.ts), so each face's event store and archive name the other face, and a bundle cut from either carries that naming; C1's second cut runs the hand-off only where a share entry names the pair. Today every hot and cold custody record seals under one S key (vault-carriers.ts, browser-custody.ts), so face isolation reads as routing alone until this lands. Item K15 carries the build, and F6 slice three lands with its step 3. How a vessel-common act unlocks dissolves (next ruling).
  • A shared act never makes a shared secret (operator ruling, 2026-10-19; four ratcheted research rounds, lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/research/custody-per-face-2026 ). Every act that touches more than one face sorts one of three ways: its private bytes DISSOLVE into the owning face's records (D), its public bytes stand at the FLOOR by design (F), or it names a vessel SECRET (S) whose principal and opener the house names record by record. A mixed carrier splits before it sorts. The browser holds no vessel key: founding, fleet join, slot changes, roster, archives, handle book, dyads, restore and clear sort D or F, so each tab asks only its own face's unlock and public floor work asks nothing. Phones, installed web apps, probes, the CLI, MCP and watcher seats and a native shell hold no S. A tablet waking floor carries ciphertext and public records unattended and decrypts no face's record. A container restarts at its floor and nothing unlocks it unattended. A guest island that owns a key store stands as its own vessel. A node holds S only for records it names: the serving key has no record yet, so it guards nothing today. * The place signs by a scoped signer under a pre-rotated KEL (operator ruling, 2026-10-19; a fifth research round, lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/research/custody-per-face-2026 #/the-place-signer). A node's place identity keeps a KEL whose inception commits the next key, and that next key rides the founding card beside the seed charter's cold next key, offline; the online place signer runs unattended for an enumerated allowlist of floor duties alone (the KEL's own events excepted, which the card's key signs), rests under the host's TPM-bound credential where the host offers one and an owner-only file elsewhere, and never rides an environment, an image or a log. A stolen online signer rotates away by the card; a lost card beside a stolen signer re-founds. The floor holds no custody key; it holds its own named place signer. The format change rides the founding bake (X0). The place KEL commits its next cold key on the founding card and rotates by that card (W29), and the seed census names every reach of the seed past the floor, K15's owed bonds among them (tools/seed-census.mjs).
  • Custody and identity read by capability, never by host (operator ruling, 2026-10-19: "I consider node and browser isomorphic-by-intent. Eventually, a browser-vessel somewhere may grow comparable capabilities to a node vessel, and this pattern cascades outwards to all future vessels."). Three rules hold on every vessel, present and planned: a vessel meets each face through that face's atom alone, its veil signing every dial, join, enrolment, sibling channel and daemon proof made on that face's behalf, never one device-wide key; a vessel holds a place signer exactly while it stands a floor, for the floor's allowlisted duties, under the pre-rotated place KEL; and a faceless vessel reading another's floor proves nothing private, reading the public shelf under the floor card's pin. A node's daemon auth, device-key signer, sibling enrolment and group-secret opener read the one vessel seed today (open-node-vessel.ts), and a browser's do the same (open-browser-vessel.ts); each is a bond owed, carried by K15. The lend follows the same rule: one lend-and-wear contract rests in the mesh (packages/lararium-mesh/src/lent-faces.ts, rank 2b), and each host binds its own light (the pūlama on the node, the tab's custody ask on the browser) and its own lend source (the pūlama's admission on the node, PersonaGroup membership on the browser), with no type or port naming a host.
  • A page never stands as a vessel (operator ruling, 2026-10-29: "Approve your leans", after the mirror-thread talk-story). Every page holds a SURFACE with a port onto a vessel, in every setup: a browser on a node's own box, an anon-device browser vessel, a fleet of equivalent vessels. The no-VM-on-the-main-thread law reads "no sovereign VM and no key on a surface" ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/api/lararium-canonical-model #/the-laws), and the DreamDOM mirror, a camera carrying a whole engine and no truth, honors it ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/dreamdom #/open-rulings). Custody follows: the anon-device browser vessel keeps its opened key in its own worker, never in page memory (the per-tab ask stands, and the shared holder still carries no unlock); it seals its store through sealed-store steps 2 and 3; and it takes an unlock ceremony the surface cannot paint, the boundary sitting where the person types the secret (its shape stays research). The full mirror runs people's plugins only after those seal steps land for the browser store; no clear record stands under the full mirror, and the device key rests non-extractable. Browser persona roots already rest sealed once a slot binds (browser-custody.ts); only the place seed and veil archives may stand clear before a slot, and the opened tree key in page memory names the exposure this ruling moves.
  • Identity enacts a bond (operator ruling, 2026-10-19). Lineage stays within a root, and covenant joins roots: a root (a compartment seed, a vessel key, a face custody key, a charter seat, a content key) derives children for its own duties alone, and any identity spanning two roots is a bond, an act both ends sign that seats a fresh key of its own, resting with the end whose privacy it serves and lapsing by its own act; no closure holds a root ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/identity-enacts-relation #/identity-as-bond, its seven laws and bond table).
  • A face's device veil is its own random key (operator ruling, 2026-10-19, re-reading the 2026-09-05 veil ruling). Each PersonaGroup's veil on a device mints at random when the device joins that face and rests under that face's FCK, so no device seed stands as an ancestor of any face's veil; it stays vessel-local, never presents, and reads mint-shaped to every observer. The group-secret opener follows it.
  • Each realm stands under one charter, a seed's or a Nexus's (operator ruling, 2026-10-18). A council that meets at one hearth stands under that hearth's seed charter, and its guests' chosen faces walk there with no Nexus founded; a council two hearths carry stands under the Nexus those two found fresh, since a Nexus names the operators' relationship ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/kapae #/edge-hands). A guest sees every realm its worn face stands in.
  • A write lands on a working layer, and canon takes it by promotion (operator ruling, 2026-10-18). A LOAD lands on a wiki's working slot alone; canon takes content only by the cap-gated promotion MOVE ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/wiki-layer-ontology ), and genesis and founding seed canon through their own named door. An act aimed at a wiki no island mounts asks the vessel to mount it, and refuses gracefully, naming the cause, when the mount cannot stand. Item W27 carries the build.
  • Every bag opens as its own wiki (operator ruling, 2026-10-18). The floor recipe names the bag alone over @oracle, derived and never minted, and an absent record reads as that floor, never as "no such wiki"; a composed wiki's record, which a hand authors, lays several bags and plugin offerings beneath the wiki's own canon bag ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/wiki-layer-ontology #/quine). No act enumerates bags to mint wikis. Item W27 carries the build.
  • One owner per registry; the floor reads by CID (operator ruling, 2026-10-18: "Architecture stands, leans stand"). The @oracle document as a registry every vessel writes retires: one id carried one lares and one lararium pointer for the whole fleet, and a joined vessel's pointer replaced its hearth's, a shape a two-vessel probe and three shard runs measured. @oracle stands as the floor's read-face over a manifest CID, which a wiki mounts read-only by the pin its vessel accepted. Each registry carries only what its scope's owner writes: a vessel's bootstrap holds its own document pointers and its control home its accepted floor pins; @catalog the operator's documents; the Kahu realm the live lares and lararium documents and their drafts; the Nexus's offer table, beside that realm, the signed offers any face makes toward canon (the house's decision, 2026-11-13, overrulable: the road takes the built door's form; lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/offering#/the-canon-offer ); the crossroads board the Nexus's one public answer. The leans the ruling adopts: the Kahu realm holds the live system documents, with bags/ projected on the Kahu's vessel alone; a one-seat founding lands a counted 1-of-1 Kahu-cabal admission for persona zero's group, so one predicate decides every hand's reach into system canon; the release answer, the Nexus's one public answer, names the Nexus AID, the charter epoch, the release before it and the floor manifest CID, sealed by the steward, with the promotion approval over the manifest's exact bytes riding attached; a vessel records an accepted release as one pin per (vessel, Nexus) in its control home that only a person's act moves (operator ruling 2026-11-11: the vessel pins the floor, faces pin nothing); genesis bakes the first floor, a corpus CID in the seed. A signing domain of its own carries the release, floor-release, so no seal over another shape verifies as one (operator-approved, 2026-10-18). The shape and its slices stand at lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/w26-floor-lane ; items W26, W21 and P8 carry the build.

Operator rulings, 2026-10-31

  • Recovery contacts, custodians and the Handle (operator ruling, 2026-10-31: "Approve recovery contact, and during this rewrite we should use Handle (& explain it) over 'name'."). A person who holds a recovery key over one face's key history and signs its rotation reads as a recovery contact, the field's own word for the role. A quorum of a face's recovery contacts rotates the face's root beneath its fixed KEL prefix, so the face keeps its Handle: the public, self-certifying card a face publishes and others recognise by its own key, never by a registry and never by a pet-name ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/heraldry #/ruling). A holder of one share of a root seed reads as a custodian (CustodianTag already says so). A custodian card returns memory, a recovery contact returns the Handle, and neither stands in for the other. Guardian keeps only its human sense (a child's legal guardian) and the fiction (the Lares as guardian spirits), and the Kahu keep their own name. Text a person reads says "your Handle" where it means what recovery restores, never "your name". The rename across canon, code and the CLI rides one lane, with a weld test before it.
  • Two of three recovery contacts (operator ruling, 2026-10-31: "2 of 3"). A face enrols exactly three recovery contacts at threshold two: one lost contact leaves recovery standing, and no single contact can rotate the face alone. Enrolment refuses any other count or threshold by name. The face's own self-recovery digest counts as none of the three (operator, 2026-10-31, approving the house lean): it keeps its own road, a seed in hand rotating the face alone, so the quorum that can rotate a face never includes a key the person lost. Item K18 carries the door.
  • The memory-stage hearth comes first (operator ruling, 2026-10-31: "I need a locally working node+browser joined (all vessels start at anon floors, so the full rites), so you all can stop using md in an untracked scratch dir & start using the daemon wiki as a 'memory-stage' (see neuroscience + Ai developments) for all design tracking and self improvement feedback loops.").   • The working surface is the repo checkout (operator ruling, 2026-10-31: "Approve (b), the working surface IS the SDM repo checkout after all (but this should be configurable for other operators or myself to do a 'non-dev install' later)."). Operator rulings, 2026-11-04

The face grant key stays sealed while custody stands unlocked; each ward-gated act unlocks it fresh from the passphrase through the minted KDF port (native scrypt on the host, noble in a worker, identical bytes), signs once and zeroes it in finally. The unlock runs in the vessel's worker, never on a page.
  • The co-sign binds by reach and ignores vessel class. The cross-device co-sign binds acts whose effect leaves the device (kapae-lower, the house cap, push lei); a local promote-commit needs the fresh unlock alone. A device holds its own atom and custody, so a browser vessel and a node vessel count as two even on one metal, and two tabs of one vessel count as one. The ward reads devices and reach, never class, so a browser-only user fleet binds the same way; a one-device fleet needs the fresh unlock alone until a second device enrolls. A lost co-signing device pauses the cross-reaching acts until re-enrollment through the recovery rite.

Operator rulings, 2026-11-05

  • Flow to the standing (operator: "Pono, Approve. FLOW to the standing of the first live Lararium."). The founding line (#/the-order, rank 32): S2's marker cure, S2b the browser ward, S3-pre-b the founding and join wiring of the per-face vessel tag, S3b the gate hook and the CI container (landed, PR #164) precede the rite, and S4 to S6 follow the standing. The house ruled S3-pre-b founding-critical under that delegation: a founding admission without the tag would refuse under the later law, and the only cure would re-found.
  • Kahua lifts stay warded (operator, after the four-scale talk-story). At every scale the reach law holds: the working top serves as the free local floor the AI writes on, and a lift crosses devices, since the stage syncs to every device the face wears, so it takes the face's own hand, one passphrase per verified batch (L21), never an operator's or a temple's. Memory steers the spirit (the Lares read the kahua at cold boot), so it carries weight the way code does. Civic and shared stages compose several warded hands by threshold, and each plane's count rests on its own civic ruling (unbuilt). The ward travels with the face across temples with no registry. The batch lift serves as the household's feeding rite, its cadence wholly the person's own, with no timer and no nudge. This amends the 2026-11-04 line that named every lift free; the lift.capability-tension loop closes for the daemon and kahua wikis with the ward ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/dreamnet-architecture#/faces-fleets-and-the-ward ).
  • Fleets overlap, with one worn face per device or session (operator; trued to the lit set, 2026-11-11 below). A human holds plural faces, each its own fleet, together a private cabal of one; one vessel may stand enrolled in several and keep several OPEN, each sealing under its own key, while it wears ONE, and no record links a human to a device. Distinctness reads by the face's own atoms: a co-sign answer counts only when its signer sits in that face's device set and differs from the asker. A vessel's key never enters a face admission, since that would link a person's faces. A per-face blinded vessel tag, HMAC(tag secret, face group) signed by the per-face tag key that HMAC seeds and unlinkable across faces, catches a vessel admitted twice into one face. The tag secret mints at random on the device, one per face, at the founding (the founder's face) or at the join request (the joinee's face), and rests in that face's own D record, vessel-local (bundle: stays): no place, floor or root seed derives it, so a stolen place seed computes no face's tag, a re-join on the same face presents the same tag, and a place rotation leaves every face's tag standing. The admission also carries the device's per-face WARD KEY (wardKey, under the tag key's signature; operator, 2026-11-06, approving the house's defaults): the public half of the key a fresh ward unlock of that face on that device opens, derived from the face's own ward key on a node and minted at random per face on a browser, so it links no two faces either, and beside it that key's own proof of possession (wardPop, its signature over (group, atom, ward key) under ward-key-pop, signed inside a fresh ward unlock), so a device that names another's key seats nothing; a co-sign approve counts only beside that key's signature over (the answer, the ask's digest, the fleet frontier the answerer read), and a rewrap changes the ward's wrapping, never that key. A memory moving between faces counts as a carry and needs both faces' hands. Remote lifts ride the intent plane: a proposal posts by digest with its body onto the face-private plane for review and loads as nothing, one warded lift intent lands on any vessel of the face, and the vessel holding the bytes verifies and moves them locally. Owed: a guard so no loader takes a proposal body on the plane for memory.
  • The ward's reach (operator and house, talk-story). Every act that reads the persona-root seed and changes who belongs to the face or what the face trusts becomes a ward act: admit, fleet-join, recovery-contact enrolment, handle and nexus publication, the founder certificate, the seal ceremony and a holder's edge kāpae, beside the bound acts of 2026-11-04. A root-reading act measured to run unattended moves to the device-atom signature under the face grant chain and prompts for nothing. One prompt per act, never cached. A face founded before the ward refuses bound acts by name with its cure, and nothing rewraps silently or reads the old shape. The operator's real face is born with its ward key at the fresh founding. F11's vault and admit verbs ride the same ward.
  • Item 7's house calls (S3 to S6, talk-story, 2026-11-05). The swarm holders start through a ward act; a seat's reach to the unix socket gets assumed until S4 measures it; the co-signing device fresh-unlocks too; set-aside devices count toward the device total and never co-sign; with no capability descriptor the relay refuses at start and writes nothing; the receipt trust root names the daemon's vessel key as a residual; the house cap and lane shares sync fleet-wide on the face-private board, while go, roster and receipts stay device-local. The design rests in lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/seat-harness#/the-swarm-slices .
  • The act ward (operator asked for the full floor). On the daemon and kahua wikis, an action string runs in a throwaway copy of the wiki, its recorded diff is judged, and a clean diff applies as itself; a code-bearing meme_act there refuses outright until the ward stands. User wikis run acts live with full caps. The design rests in lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/kahua-memory-stage#/the-hearth-lane .

The operator approved the house's defaults (operator, 2026-11-06: "Approve your default leans so far."). Each bullet below cites "operator, 2026-11-06, approving the house's defaults", and a cold Luna read held each against the memegraph's laws first, its note folded in.

  • The ward key rides the admission (operator, 2026-11-06, approving the house's defaults). Each device's per-face ward public key rides its signed admission (wardKey, covered by vesselSig, the tag key's signature over group, atom and ward key) beside its proof of possession (wardPop), and a ward-act approval carries that key's signature over the answer, the ask's digest and the frontier the answerer read; the vessel key never enters a face admission (the fleets-overlap ruling, 2026-11-05). An approval binds one ask's digest, and the frozen spend refuses its replay. A ward key never rotates silently: a rewrap keeps the key, and a device whose ward goes missing re-joins as a new vessel (ward-key-would-rotate). The law rests in lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/seat-harness#/the-swarm-slices .
  • The asker's session seals (operator, 2026-11-06, approving the house's defaults; it sharpens "the asker seals" of 2026-10-19 above). The asker's session seals, on the vessel worker's thread, while that face's session stands unlocked, and seal-awaits-session stands. When the asker cannot answer, another admitted, unlocked session of the same face takes the seal, and the takeover refuses once no session of that face stands unlocked. Carriers: lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/k15-wave-b-lane#/the-reds , and pono/browser-vessel-lift, carried by the b1 lanes' carriers at their land.
  • Removal wins at every reader (operator, 2026-11-06, approving the house's defaults; from the edge-frontier ruling "a raise shadows its target at once for every reader that sees it"). A reader whose own fleet frontier counts a device set aside refuses that device's outcomes and its certificate alike: the gate's certificate check and the reader's outcome check both refuse it, and a reader whose frontier has not yet counted the set-aside reads against its last verified view ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/face-proof#/the-offline-root ). A refused cap tip reads the tightest cap that ever stood on its line (braked). The law rests in lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/seat-harness#/the-swarm-slices .

The disk projector writes a raised kāpae onto disk as <stem>.kapae and ingest raises it again, so a re-pour of an old export never brings a burned meme back (L34 keeps the residues). (d) Two memories that choose one readable key settle by authoring discipline (L19's class and slug) and placeMeme's base-hash refusal, with no new mechanism. Stage memes stay in the kahua working projection under the kept state root (cleartext only on disk and in the live wiki), never pushed to git; durability past this machine rides the vault's backup door, as today's flat memories rest outside git.

Operator rulings, 2026-11-09 and 2026-11-10 ~ the placement leap and the locked floor

  • Placement over patterns (operator, 2026-11-09, the placement leap). Where a record rests decides where it reaches disk: a record projects by the bag that holds it and that bag's mirror grant, never by its title or a field it carries; a record that must stay off disk rests where no mirror reaches (the control document, the secret home, the shadow layer, a board no recipe mounts). lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/api/disk-projection#/projection-filter ; tools/placement-residency-witness.mjs reads it.
  • Parity across vessel types (operator, 2026-11-09: "the system get idempotent-by-intent and isomorphic-by-intent partity between all vessel types."). A host-shaped deviation never rides as a filed item: it cures before its leap lands, behind one shared helper, so node, browser and the CLI's direct holder cannot drift.
  • A locked hearth fails closed (operator, 2026-11-09). Every act that needs the vessel's control document refuses vessel-locked until custody opens; the custody door (unlock, lock, census) stays carved out; no receipt claims durability over memory; a refused unlock says why. lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/waking-floor#/the-shape .
  • The secret home and the control document mint with the face (operator, 2026-11-09). A face's founding or admission mints both under one custody record; a place (a herm, a hearth before its face) mints neither, and its control stand-in refuses control-home-unnamed.
  • One control document, face-held (operator, 2026-11-09). Every control family rests in the one control document; nothing stands a second control home.
  • The carriage log, and a sixth floor duty (operator, 2026-11-09). A place's relay state rests in its public carriage log: append-only, unsealed, keyed by the carriage's own id, each receipt signed under the place key as the sixth floor duty, CARRIAGE_RECEIPT_DOMAIN (place-signer.ts), herm and hearth alike. lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/lamplighters#/the-carriage-log .
  • A herm never unlocks (operator, 2026-11-09: "Herm(s) stand as a Laraium wihtout any unlocked "operator private" capabilities. A quine-relay."). No herm path waits on, or fakes, an unlock; whatever a herm's relay needs rests in homes that need no operator-private custody. Measured 2026-11-10: the unlock door still opens a faceless place's vessel key and stands it sovereign (custody-frames.ts unlock, node-vessel-identity.ts sovereignStands), and the herm lift walks that unlock (tests/e2e/herm-floor.test.ts R5); a named refusal stays owed behind the controller's word.
  • Both confirmations, with a Mu (operator, 2026-11-10: "Approve both confirmations; but we shall MU both after the Lararium stands and we can dogfood the design from inside the system."). The two: a herm's or locked floor's daemon wiki writes rest in memory alone and drop at recompose; the carriage log takes the sixth floor duty (a peer cosignature and a non-inclusion proof stay owed). Both re-open once the dogfood Lararium stands.
  • A re-founding keeps the projections (operator, 2026-11-08, the survive-refound leap). One named house root; a re-founding clears hearth/ alone, and projections/ survives it, private cleartext memory included.
  • Plain text survives to disk (operator, 2026-11-10: "The state of play now is 'Dogfood our code into a self-improving AI-Operator alignment harness' so some things must wait. The 'plain text survives to disk' law holds because how do we recover running data between alpha refound otherwise (& this lesson carries forwards, etc)."). The plaintext projection survives every re-founding by design, so running data recovers across alpha re-foundings (lares export and import, a reset that keeps projections/), and the law carries forward past alpha. Work beyond the truths it needs waits behind the dogfood harness. lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/api/disk-projection#/core-claim .

Operator rulings, 2026-11-11 ~ the house canon, the vessel pin, the workbench and the lit set

  • House canon rests at contract; its CIDs read public (operator, 2026-11-11: "veil ⊂ persona-group ⊂ contract ⊂ public -> Those note the 'access posture' for CRDTs, and the CRDTs for the canon rest under Kahu cabal contract.
