  • The vessel pins the floor (operator, 2026-11-11: "The pull/push tension can go the way the memegraph/code leans"). A release gets cut and published; each VESSEL accepts it, one pin per (vessel, Nexus) in its control home, and faces pin nothing. The accept fast-forwards along prior and refuses a fork or a non-descendant by name; an island that swaps its floor live swaps, any other raises the reboot alert. lares vessel floor status|accept. Landed (63bc695d9, W26 slice 5; lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/w26-floor-lane#/the-vessel-pin ).
  • Faces form a private cabal of one (operator, 2026-11-11: "the multitude of faces reads as a private cabal for 1 human. Pattern integrities!"). One human's faces sharing a bag or a wiki enact the cabal grammar at the scale of one person: the charter names the share, the membership names the faces, and no parallel ACL stands beside it. An unshared wiki founds a private cabal of one (privateCabalRealmId(face, slug)). OWED: the share config ($:/config/lares/share/<slug>, share-config.ts) still resolves face-to-face shares through its own resolver, beside the cabal's read scope.
  • Contracted members sync the working layer (operator, 2026-11-11: "We want contracted members to have synced access to the 'working layer' too, even cross operator."). A shared wiki's working layer rests as one realm-workbench CRDT that every member its contract seats syncs; edits merge in the CRDT, and canon stays promotion-gated. Landed for one operator's faces ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/cabal-realm#/the-workbench ); the cross-operator join stays OWED at the realm's member consult.
  • Personal carries the private margin (operator, 2026-11-11: "Personal can absorb the new caps."). personal holds one face's private margin over the shared workbench as well as its view state, crosses that face's own devices alone, and projects per face to personal/<F>/<slug>, seeding back. Landed ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/api/disk-projection#/personal ).
  • The full new design, no lift (operator, 2026-11-11: "Unpono, rip out old code, enact the full new design, early-alpha law holds."). No per-face working document stands for any wiki but the daemon's: every other wiki's working layer rests in a realm workbench at realmWorkbenchDocUrl(realm, slug); the daemon wiki stays face-private; workspaces bind at /lar/workspaces/<name>; the recipe reads canon -> workbench -> personal -> draft -> temp. Landed on the node (the founding, found-hearth.ts; lares persona, persona.ts). The browser writes the label at its founding too, into OPFS (browser-face-labels.ts, one shared seam with the node).

The recipe stack alone decides what shows: a working record shadows canon's, nothing retracts a higher layer, and a seed's canon leg warns where canon stands fresher (override-bases.ts, seed-mirrors.ts). Landed.

Operator rulings, 2026-11-12 ~ the veil's upgrade, the leaf fleet, the route door and the next two leaps

  • A join upgrades the veil, never splits it (operator, 2026-11-12: "The concepts called for an anon face acting to join a PersonaGroup fleet as an upgraded capability, not as a seperate A+B faces."). One composer and one seat core serve every vessel, and a host differs only in what its capabilities carry ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/device-capabilities-2026#/device-seats , its in-house rulings 1 to 10; the stories in lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/dreamnet-architecture#/the-walkers ).

  • Three leans approved (operator, 2026-11-12: "Pono, approve all three leans. Update any memes that read un-pono.").
    1. A leaf binds hearthTrueName: "" by design. The field names a hearth's seed-charter Nexus AID; a hearth-agnostic leaf states it empty, no place wall stands for a leaf, and the AID's wall arrives by an act when a face founds or joins a hearth ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/lararium-identity#/delegation-edge ). The node stands its veil floor, joins the face F through the carriage (the leaves' seat door, with seat verbs on the node's command line), and seals the seed charter as F; today found forces a fresh face 0 ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/founding-runbook#/the-growth-rite ).

A leaf at its veil floor that offers a fleet join holds no face yet; the offer act founds the face first, through the same one-boot ask the identity panel's founding door carries, and then offers it. A vessel that stands in several Nexuses holds one floor pin per Nexus, and its recipe stacks those floors innermost-first: its Household's canon shadows the Crossroads', which shadows the Temple's, with the planetary floor beneath; the top layer shadows across tiers as it does within one ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/w26-floor-lane#/many-floors ). Operator rulings, 2026-11-13 ~ the confirm gate for critical procedures, and the swarm's re-think

  • Critical procedures take a confirm gate (operator, 2026-11-13: "Note for the future (find the DreamNet/DreamDom roadmaps and survey then true up and add) that critical procedures like lares("vessel", "reset") and its UX twin should get gated behind an operator/user confirmation (like the pwd, etc)."). A critical procedure and its page twin stand behind a confirmation of the operator or user who holds the device. The browser's lares("vessel", "reset") and the page's own button raise one reset ask, and the ceremony document burns the place only on the pass its confirm gate mints ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/browser-vessel-lift#/the-confirm-gate ; C4 in #/the-leaps). The host shim's reset takes a typed word alone (C10). The gate's shape rests in the house's lean below, overrulable (#/open).
  • The gate's shape (the house's lean, talked out 2026-11-13; overrulable, and never the operator's ruling). The house weighed the note against the anon-floor law, under which every vessel MUST always return to its floor ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/dreamnet-rhizome #/the-ground), and against the ward's bound acts ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/dreamnet-architecture#/faces-fleets-and-the-ward ), and leans to four lines; the browser stands built as leaned, and the fourth line's census reaches the browser's burn primitives alone.
    1. Custody held asks the passphrase. Where the vessel holds custody (a first passphrase set, or a face joined), the reset asks that passphrase through the ceremony document before anything burns.
    2. A bare floor takes the typed phrase. At a bare floor with nothing sealed, the typed phrase suffices.
    3. No house door gates the platform. A holder who lost the passphrase keeps the platform's own site-data clear, which no house door can gate, so the return to the floor stays whole.
    4. The class reads wider than reset. Any door that destroys a place or a face on a device, on any vessel, takes the gate by default, and so does its UI twin.
  • Answered with the gate: a locked vessel verifies a passphrase without unlocking, by the unwrap alone against the trees at rest; the reset proves it in the ceremony document's own worker with the vessel worker stood down, and holds no key by design. The lean above flips on the operator's word.
  • The swarm's procedures re-think once the Lararium stands (operator, 2026-11-13: "We shall have to completely re-think our multi-model & swarm procedures once we have the fully standing Lararium."). The multi-model and swarm procedures ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/mixed-model-swarm , the lane law of controller-handoff) take a complete re-think once the Lararium fully stands; until then they hold as written. Owed in #/the-leaps, after the walk.

House rulings under the operator's delegation ~ the questions the operator delegated, each with what flips it

The operator delegated these to the house: each stands as a house ruling the operator may overturn, its law lives in the carrier it names, and its build rides the stride items #/the-loops indexes.

  • The ember's key at rest (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). A separate serving key (SK), the fifth custody class serving, wrapped by the vessel key and by ember slots, seals the serving records alone, and the serving tier holds no minting secret: a walker's grant checks under a public half, a shape the hosted road's re-cut lands before the ember's walker arm. An unattended answer (any slot that answers without a person's confirm on the ember) needs a freshness anchor a disk copy cannot carry, a hardware-sealed hearth key whose attestation the ember checks on each ask; without one every ember slot answers only on a person's confirm, threshold two buys a second confirm and never an unattended raise, and after any counted removal a bare deny-only sk-rotation-owed mark refuses every ask until SK rolls, a mark a rolled-back disk copy can omit. For the operator: a hearth that holds no hardware anchor raises only on a person's confirm. The reason: a key that opens serving state opens nothing beside it, and a copied disk replays any freshness it carries; the ruling flips on a threat review showing the serving records hold nothing a thief can use even before the grant re-cut ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/custody#/the-table ; lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/ember ).
  • The ember's serving gate (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). A third gate tier, the serving route, takes its bundle from SK, and the floor and sovereign tiers stand untouched; a pin moves only by a signed causal event or a verified key-history step, and no quiet period ages a pin out. The reason: a dialer that pinned the sovereign tier never mistakes a serving hearth for an unlocked one. The ruling flips on proof that a gate pin decides no authority anywhere ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/face-proof#/the-gate-exchange ).
  • How a raise lapses (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). A raise lives one boot and lapses at the next causal boundary (a lock, a restart, a human unlock, a counted removal), and that boundary IS the expiry the waking floor requires; no timer and no heartbeat ends a standing, and the count spends at the raise ledger's durable commit before SERVING composes, so a crash before the commit spends nothing and serves nothing and a crash after it burns the raise. The reason: quiet never tells a slow device from a gone one; the residue: a rolled-back ledger on a disk copy restores spent raises. The ruling flips on a causal renewal event every reader receives that also survives the ember's loss ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/waking-floor#/the-shape ).
  • A removal no reader can keep (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). The based removal refuses by name where no parallel-drafts reader keeps the concurrent words, or every reader failed, and the store authors nothing; the refusal carries a durable conflict handle (the document, the removal's request id, the conflicting change and head identities, never the hand's text), so a hand whose editor went offline finds the same conflict on its return. The reason: a draft the store wrote would choose a bag, a tier and a steward no hand named. The ruling flips on an already-authorized destination for such words that keeps provenance and tier without the store choosing either ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/automerge-doc-store ).
  • The outbox at rest (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). Each outbox parcel seals under randomized authenticated encryption with a stable outbox key the vessel key wraps and an unlock hands the daemon island, the way content keys reach it, and rests under a random name with its routing inside the sealed body, so a locked disk names no reader; the sealed-outbox carrier leaves floor-plain for a sealed class. Before a parcel's atomic commit nothing stands sendable, after it the parcel re-opens and retries at the next unlock, and after a ship and before the local delete the receiver folds a repeat idempotently. The reason: a reader digest on a stolen disk names a relationship; no flip stands expected, since the ruling closes a residue ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/custody#/the-table ).
  • The herm relay leg (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). The ask rides the sealed sibling session under that session's seal with a tagged inner frame, and the delivery receipt stays a bare session note good for the asker alone, bound to a unique request id and the session's causal position, attesting transit and never execution; reach narrows to one PersonaGroup, and the leg lands with W9. The reason: the fewest new shapes, and no signing domain minted for a transport fact. The ruling flips on a concrete need for a third party to audit relay delivery after the asker vanishes ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/receipt-ledger#/the-relay-leg ).
  • The disk watcher's reach (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). A pending removal settles with no person present only under a standing instruction the operator signs by a human route, naming one bag and a bounded class of removals (a path pattern and a per-wave count ceiling): the watcher executes within it, every settled removal lands in the audit with the instruction it rode, and lares shore restore still answers it. An automated seat never signs one, a standoff (disk and records differ) never settles without a person, and a mass vanish past the instruction's ceiling stays pending for a person. The reason: a person who answered in writing for exactly that class has answered, and a count alone proves no intent; the ruling flips on a single incident where an in-scope instruction deleted what a person meant to keep ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/api/disk-projection#/projection-law ).
  • The restore's key history (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). A restored face stands PROVISIONAL until at least one party to its relation (a sibling device of the same human, a seated chair of its Nexus, or a recovery contact) shows its own log view and that view agrees with the restore's head; while provisional the face reads, recovers and serves its own content and signs no authority-changing act, the restore prints which head it read and from where, and the provisional state names who could confirm it. Where every such party stays unreachable, the recovery contacts' path (the impersonation quorum) answers, never the local read alone. The reason: no vessel reads a head it never received, and the house keeps no registry of heads; no flip stands, and the residue stays stated: one agreeing party may itself sit stale ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/custody#/the-restore ).
  • The Offering Antigen scope (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). A signed Offering act carries a stable scope, the joint Nexus AID its signed inception mints, beside sealEpochCid as the authorizing epoch. The receiver resolves its own charter scope, verifies the act's epoch inside that scope's locally held lineage, checks the quorum at that exact epoch, and only then folds; an act whose scope differs stays as foreign evidence outside the kapae and un_kapae fold, and an incomplete lineage reads unavailable. The joint inception image commits to the offer fingerprint and to both founders' fresh cold successor commitments, and a successor commitment a vault already used refuses, so the AID names one founding statement and two foundings by one roster never alias. The reason: a neighbour's condemnation copied onto another board reads as testimony, never as that board's verdict. The ruling flips on a case where two foundings by one roster must read as one Nexus ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/research/offering-antigen-scope-decision-record ).
  • The fork-resolve act (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). A fork stays a fork, and every reader names the branch it follows; no clock, no longest chain and no third-party quorum picks a branch. Two acts resolve one. ABANDON: a seal record a branch's own quorum signs at its head, closing that branch for every reader who holds it, erasing nothing and touching no sibling. REUNION: one canonical image naming the scope AID, both held heads sorted, and the new seats, threshold and successor commitments, signed by each branch's quorum at its head over identical bytes; the verifier proves both heads inside the same AID's lineages from a common ancestor, and the record becomes the common head. A seal record's predecessors form a sorted set of one or two, built before the fence; until the reunion verifier lands, a two-predecessor record refuses REUNION_UNBUILT. The reason: a split household reunites only by a paper both halves sign. The ruling flips on a fork that neither branch's quorum can still sign for.
  • The re-seal proof (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). When a content key rolls at a counted removal, the face session that writes each replacement signs one causal re-seal record per replacement: the bag locator, the old and new key-version ids, the old and new ciphertext addresses, the logical entry it replaces and the removal act that authorized the roll. The removal quorum signs the roll, never each body. A reader with partial history verifies the signature and every binding it holds, and reports a missing parent as unverified, never as complete. The format lands with sealed-store step three, before the fence. The reason: a phone offline through the roll needs to know which photo replaced which, and whose hand re-sealed it. The ruling flips on proof that the key-version record, manifests and checkpoints already show every replacement and omission to both kinds of reader ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/sealed-store ).
  • Recovery contact enrolment's shape (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). Each face founds one-of-one with no recovery contacts, and its log's verifier knows a typed first-enrolment event from the start: the face's own current key signs it, it commits the recovery contact set and threshold, and the face's prefix stays as it stands; every later change to the set takes the recovery contacts' authority. The event shape and its verifier land before the fence, so an enrolment never re-founds a face. The reason: a face founded alone keeps its Handle's history when recovery contacts join a year later, and no third hand holds standing over a one-of-one face. The ruling flips on an existing event kind that already permits that first, face-key-signed transition. The count stands ruled at two of three recovery contacts (#/rulings, 2026-10-31); request authentication, the veto, death and succession stay open (#/open).
  • Engine cadence (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). Cadence keeps its own lineage and threshold, apart from every seat key. A Nexus's joint founding cites one cadence inception that names each founder's hearth, the device that bears the reboot, as a holder; a household seed carries none, since its one holder bears its reboots. Each hearth mints its cadence key at random on the device, never derived from serving or floor material, and keeps it, with its next-key commitment, as a cold record under the vessel key that unwraps per act; no sixth custody class and no extra card at the founding. A holder may later rotate onto a cold card by its own choice. Each island pins one engine epoch, and one daemon hosts two epochs only as two separately pinned islands. The reason: the people who bear a reboot decide it, and a seat-holder who runs no hardware forces no one's restart. The ruling flips on a cadence act that must run unattended where a per-act unwrap cannot ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/kahu#/contract ).
  • A realm's quorum and its boards (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-14). Each realm stands under ONE Nexus, which the vessel's first act there names and its edge ledger keeps; every reader takes the realm's quorum roster off that Nexus's charter, never off its own primary one; and the realm's boards key on the realm's own id, so a removal's record stays among its parties. The reason: authority comes from the governing charter while the record addresses the relation it affects. The ruling flips on a realm-native charter model with its own seat creation, migration and conflict rule ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/kapae#/edge-hands ).
  • A restore after recovery contact enrolment (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). A face's own bundle restores after it enrols recovery contacts, so long as the face's log still holds the set its own root enrolled by the typed first event and no later event moved it; a recovery contact rotation that moves the set cuts every earlier bundle off. The restored face stands provisional as the restore's key-history ruling holds. The reason: enrolling recovery contacts must never silently kill a person's own backups, and the recovery contacts' rotation, not the enrolment, cuts off a stolen bundle. The ruling flips on a provisional gate that cannot stop a restored face from signing an authority-changing act before a party confirms it ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/custody#/the-restore ).
  • Paper restores a root, never memory (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). The split-root cards rebuild the persona root alone; content keys stay random and wrap under the vessel key or a bundle's own key, and no key the root derives opens memory. A person recovers memory through a bundle, under the route that bundle seals. The reason: a root card that opened memory would turn every share holder and every thief of the paper into a reader of the face's private bytes. The ruling flips on an explicit choice that root-share holders read memory, with the custody that choice costs stated.
  • A restore as persona zero (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). A restore onto a fresh hearth runs through the founding act: the hearth founds a fresh one-seat seed charter, and the restored face stands as its persona zero and holds its new chair. A bundle's old chair claim carries no authority on the new hearth, since a bundle carries no seat key. A founding that stopped part way resumes only its own persona-zero root and its own seed charter, and refuses a root that differs from the restored one rather than minting over it. The reason: a person who lost their one machine meets their own face, never a second one. The ruling flips on a rule that a lost hearth's chair must survive, which would make a bundle carry seat authority it excludes.
  • A roll waits on its prior anchor (operator ruling, 2026-10-15). A seal roll refuses by name while the prior roll stands with no anchor, so no Nexus stacks a roll over a step its readers cannot yet carry across a removal. The reason: an admit carries across a seal roll only through its roll anchors, and a roll laid over an anchorless one strands every admit between them.
  • The working address (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15; the operator's workbench rulings of 2026-11-11 above flip it). The daemon wiki alone keeps a face-private working document. Every other wiki's working layer rests in a realm workbench at realmWorkbenchDocUrl(realm, slug), deterministicDocUrl over the realm board's address with #working/<slug>: no member change moves it and knowing it grants nothing, and an unshared wiki's realm id derives from the owning face and the slug (privateCabalRealmId), so two offline devices of one face converge on one address ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/cabal-realm#/the-workbench ).
  • The admit's gate pin (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). The admit carriage and fleet-join answer sign a validated full GateBundlePin for the hearth's sovereign sync route at the named Nexus, read by readGateBundlePin; its digest derives from that object. Dials index by (hearth, Nexus, route, tier). Floor routes receive their own full pins through an authenticated relation or a carried signed pin set; an announcement never authenticates its own first pin. The reason: a digest alone gives an offline dialer no trusted route or key. The ruling flips on an equally authenticated digest-to-full-pin carrier with an unambiguous route and offline first contact.
  • Frames after open (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). A versioned sealed application header carries kind (session, sync, or a named control kind), message id, part index and count, total length, then bytes, only after the gate seat opens. Gate tags 0x01–0x13 serve the handshake alone. Reassembly and concurrent messages carry bounds; part size follows the Noise limit after header and sealing overhead. Missing, duplicate, mixed-kind, overlong and post-close parts refuse. The reason: a large sync frame needs bounded, unambiguous carriage. The ruling flips on a smaller measured layout that passes the same large-sync, replay, memory-bound and cross-kind vectors.
  • Sharing after the gate (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). The proven same-operator class establishes transport alone. The session's proven face and scoped document view decide each document. A place arm reaches infrastructure alone; a PersonaGroup grant admits a sibling vessel of the same face and grants no blanket document access. Two tabs on one laptop wearing different faces see different books. The reason: transport kinship grants no document readership. The ruling flips on a scoped-view proof that another key carries the same face with exactly the same facets.
  • A device set aside (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). Each vessel face-key certificate binds its face key to its certified device edge. Before sorting a presentation as same-operator, the gate consults the fleet edge's shadow for that device edge and refuses while the mapping or fold reads unreadable. The wire carries only the face-scoped key. A restart and two-face witness gate the cutover. The reason: removal of a device must cut every face it wore. The ruling flips on causal withdrawal of every affected face certificate proven to reach every gate with no gap.
  • The contract seam (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). N2, N4 and N5 stand handed back. Train W owns the admit-carriage hunk of nexus-contract.ts for the cutover, keeps N's anchor and prior-roll checks, and lands the contract, device-admit.ts, fleet answer, verifier and vectors as one signed-shape change. The reason: one signature shape must cross every producer and reader together. The ruling flips on a proven independent carriage boundary that preserves those checks and signed bytes.
  • The founder's certificate (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). A founder self-issues its vessel face-key certificate through the checked issue door and withdrawal history a joiner uses. The reason: the founder's session needs the same chain and removal law. The ruling flips on a verified equivalent issue path with the same history.
  • Routes at the cutover (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). Walker arms, the sibling dial and the herm membership relay ship SHUT by name; H opens walkers with its live grant reader, W9 opens the sibling at face scope, and an explicit Nexus opens the herm relay. The oracle read face and reader convert where browser boot reads the oracle in production; elsewhere they ship SHUT by name after measuring pullAndVerifyOracle's caller. The waystone reads the Nexus's one public answer before it trusts its engine. An untested old-wire fallback counts as open. The reason: a closed route grants no silent legacy authority. The ruling flips on a proven gate-native caller for each shut route.
  • The floor announcement (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). waking-floor-announce signs one canonical body per carried Nexus under that Nexus's floor bundle signing key: { kind: "floor-announcement", nexus, floorPin, standing, prior }, prior naming the digest of the announcement this floor made before for that Nexus (null at the first). No counter and no position orders it: a reader holding an announcement takes only the one that names it as prior, and foldFloorAnnouncements names a fork rather than settling it by recency. It travels only inside an authenticated socket or relay relation. The reader checks the already pinned floor pin, domain, Nexus and route, and treats the body as a peer hint alone. The reason: a floor hint must neither bootstrap its own trust nor replay across Nexuses. The ruling flips on a smaller body that still refuses cross-Nexus replay, stale replacement and first-contact self-certification.
  • Fleet join serves every face (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-15). fleet join names its face with --face <index>, default 0; a nonzero face carries that face's certificates, grants and address secret. The reason: a person wears several faces, and each face needs its own fleet relation. The ruling flips on a face-independent proof that carries the same per-face authority without mixing identities.
  • A realm's steward map holds leaves (house ruling under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-16). The co-signed keptBy names fill realmStewardMap: each name a steward's worn per-Nexus face or a Handle, with the bags it keeps. That map answers which bags a steward keeps, never a root question; the realm consult's ROOT map stays peerContractNymMap, filled only by a root edge proven at the wire ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/membership-doctrine#/two-maps ). The reason: a leaf that answers a root question links the two maps the doctrine keeps apart. The ruling flips if a root edge proven at the reading seat comes to fill a realm map, which then stands beside this one under the root map's own name.
  • The re-cut's eight forks (house, under the operator's delegation, 2026-10-31). The cut reads left eight forks; the house talked each out two rounds against canon and a Gaia and an Elyncia story, and none survived to the operator: daemonAuth lands as one pure shared builder in mesh; node and browser pin one boot list; seven boot cells refuse a read before set by name; the share verdict lands one law per relation, measured before it merges; the stage-cut and the verb math take pure deciders and a pure module first; the shared stages converge on node's fuller form; the login suffix retires and custody resolves its home once at open; and a removed joiner never re-enters on an invite minted before the removal, its mechanism decided by the red. The floor stands at lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/test-recut-road #/the-floor, and the T items carry each settlement.
  • The channel of intent (operator, 2026-10-20: "Approve your leans, land the full models into the architecture now"). The daemon wiki keeps working memory; a face's intents travel on that face's own intent plane, @intents-<tag>, a fifth face plane beside the four; a vessel's own records rest in its daemon bag, unmirrored; the face's pins rest in the face's custody and @persona. The approved leans: the plane stands from the first landing, never staged in the daemon bag; the winner reads by the intent's own preference list, else the lowest H(atom ‖ intent id), and never elects; a single-effect fleet-wide verb never addresses "any device", and a human answer may name its executor; a claim never expires, a device's staleness stays a local suspicion, and the operator's release always wins; an every intent reaches a later-joined device unless its bound names a roster edge; a far agent reaches the plane through a relay seat, opt-in per face and ring-gated, its research before its build; an intent voids by supersession, a revoked delegation, a passed roster edge or the operator's cancel; compaction folds by the operator's act, never a timer; a local verb that touches its own vessel alone bypasses the plane and leaves its receipt in that vessel's bag. The plane carries a hand's decisions that cross devices, never its motion (the rubric, lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/face-intent-plane #/the-rubric). Item I1.
  • The place signer's fifth duty (operator, 2026-10-20, approving the controller's lean). A place's finish at another place's gate, GATE_PLACE_FINISH_DOMAIN, joins the place signer's floor duties: a faceless place that reads another's floor performs a floor act. Each of the five duties binds its domain by an exact head at byte zero, in one table beside the allowlist; X0 moves the framing into the signer.
  • Sync carries; verification decides (operator, 2026-10-20, approving the research at lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/research/identity-invariants-2026 and the controller's leans). A mutable CRDT carries identity claims and never decides them. Each face holds, on each device, a sealed identity anchor in its own custody (a D record): its PersonaGroup, its persona-KEL prefix, a commitment to its root, its device edge's digest, and a known frontier that never falls. Only founding, admission and the recovery rites (recovery contact rotation, bundle restore, root cards) write an anchor; sync never writes one. Founding and admission acts, device edges and lease advances travel as signed records in the face's @persona plane, a signed-entry log every reader verifies against the anchor and the KEL; two signed acts at one sequence surface as a fork, never a CRDT winner. The face planes hold verified projections. A device reads freely; an act that widens authority (admit, grant, re-key, seal to a reader) checks the sealed frontier and refuses a target that names a newer bound, its receipt naming the frontier it acted under. Transparency and witnesses for equivocation wait for their own item. Wave C carries the build inside K15 step 6.
  • Wave B's forks (operator, 2026-10-20, approving the controller's leans). A fresh PersonaGroup mints a fresh atom, never an orphan's seed. A tab reads its face's pins from its custody anchor before it opens its session, so the tab's own Repo retires; and each face holds its own social planes in the browser, keyed by PersonaGroup as on the node. Both ride Wave C with the anchor.
  • The order of the next waves (operator, 2026-10-20, approving the controller's leans). Wave C (K15 step 6 under the identity anchor) runs next, with W26's slices 3, 4 and 6 beside it on the floor's own ground, their composer hunks passing to the controller as patches, and slice 5 after it, since its accepted floor rests as a face's sealed record (a D record under the face's own FCK once Wave C lands); I1 follows the anchor; the far agent's relay seat takes its own research after I1; G37's renames ride X0's bake; the sensorium resumes after X0, on the operator's word.
  • The edge frontier (operator, 2026-10-20, approving the controller's leans after the research at lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/research/edge-frontier-2026 ). Nothing signed today lets a reader tell a device never set aside from a raise erased or withheld, so the house adds a signed edge frontier per board, its own domain-separated certificate type, the persona KEL resolving its signer's key. Every hand that can raise on a board signs its own stream's frontier (a fleet board's root; a realm board's charter quorum, with its holder where vouch edges raise), and a frontier commits its counted acts through its boundary as a content-addressed snapshot, its parent frontier, and its roster and epoch lineage. A raise shadows its target at once for every reader that sees it, and counts toward widening once its signer registers it into the next frontier. Sync always carries acts, frontiers and snapshots. A widening act (admit, grant, a content key sealed or re-sealed to a reader) reads its target against a frontier of every raising hand that extends any head the reader pinned and includes every raise it has seen; it acts as of that frontier, its receipt naming it, and waits as unknown where none qualifies. Every fleet and realm founds with a signed empty genesis frontier, and an admit bundle carries the current frontier and its snapshot. The fleet board's frontier stands built with Wave C, and the realm boards' leg landed with K17-R in PR #97. Item K17.

The fresh start

  • Old shapes burn (operator ruling, 2026-10-28: "O-takiage the old shapes & shape gates now. Its design time crud buildup that unfortunately causes agents to repeat these patterns. Early alpha law holds - we dont know the final shapes yet ... after founding these guards become important, so we should have clean design space to think through"). Source never recognises bytes or shapes from before a format cut, to refuse them by name or to read them tolerantly: a cut re-founds. Live refusals of today's shapes (a wrong key, a torn record, a foreign face) stand. The evergreen witness holds an old-shape class at zero; boundary guards for pinned bytes get designed after the founding, in clean space (porch bundle six, PR #109).
  • No pave or wipe command — such a command carries too much risk. See #/fresh-start.

The test refactor

  • The test architecture re-cuts before the founding (operator ruling, 2026-10-31, lifting the 2026-10-29 ruling that set it after): train T runs beside the founding road and never stalls it (#/stride-t-recut). Until each layer re-cuts, lanes land large batches and run the heavy battery once per batch.
  • The writable marker and startup order (operator ruling, 2026-11-04): the writable marker orders promoted modules after the writable route, and startup takes that marker as its gate.
  • Unsaved drafts survive a crash (operator ruling, 2026-11-04): unsaved drafts survive in CRDT state and file projection, and no TW5 syncer path carries that durability.
  • Drain pacing and request law (operator ruling, 2026-11-04): the Drain runs one latest-state queue across all hand titles, paced by durable acknowledgements and lifecycle requests, with no timer and no syncer.
  • Island capability record (operator ruling, 2026-11-04): each island opens through one record with caps.receipt, caps.cas, caps.dyad and caps.custodyWrite.
  • Code wards bind the daemon and the kahua stage, never a user's wiki (operator ruling, 2026-11-04): a StartupAction in the daemon island or a kahua-<tag> stage runs only from genesis or the promoted set; every other layer's StartupAction rides inert and names itself in the boot log. A user's own wiki keeps full caps and runs its StartupActions. The hand-gesture ward on promote-commit and kapae-lower stays a later item, and the promote hand gate (the session profile chooses its own hand) stays held for the operator.
  • Owed from the promote train's final reads (2026-11-04): the CAS holds its pins forever, since pin has no unpin; the daemon doc's promote-state snapshot grows without a bound; a draft floor's lift gives its destination no rebind between carry and burn; a receipt wait on the vessel settles on a closed channel but not on a record that withdraws receipt mid-wait (the island's teardown closes its capability first); routine daemon restart reasons (a hand's promote verb) exist on the node alone, since the browser has no routine daemon restart; a lift's in-process carry memory cannot tell a user-removed title from a lost one, so the destination-holds-it check stays unbuilt.
  • Owed from the promote train's last round (2026-11-05, the fourth Drain swing cured: a displaced whole-drain target released an act's receipt hold empty, and the pump starved a title behind one re-marked every flight): a boundary() caller's continuation stays retained while a receipt hangs, bounded by the acts that asked and released by the receipt capability's close at teardown, since no timer decides; a floor document peers declare unavailable (find rejects) wakes no lift pass, because only a peer's connection could wake it, while the seat still wakes on grant, lease and group-document changes; the rehome of a daemon-bag binding mints its fresh document before its flush, so one stall-then-recover leaves one orphan document behind; the daemon holder's restart listeners read the restart's first reason, never the emergency that upgraded it mid-prepare (no listener reads the reason today).
  • Owed after the 2026-11-05 lands (PRs #159, #160 and #162): the store door's budget timeouts (STORE_DOOR_PHASES, ten, forty-five and ten seconds) decide a refusal by wall clock, against NO GLOBAL NOW, while the recompose-window wait for place records ends on the unlock event and holds; a behavioural test for the worker's workerData path, which hands island workers their CAS dirs (session islands receive none); a herm-daemon entry in the capability inventory; the foreign-title-projection turn-order flake, narrowed by a named heuristic stability wait (50 of 50 green under load) and owed a pinned handshake; the harness suite's run.log timing case in tools/launch-codex.test.mjs ("the child's output landed in run.log through the held fd"), which reddened the span's CI once in the witness cell and joins the harness deep read; the golden-whole-road W3 flake, which a scout over its failing jobs read as a timing race on the glass port adaptor and no regression of the Drain (the Drain's diff touches waiters alone): its test half landed with the CI container (ea6e38643, the walk raises the island barrier before its click and waits the shared bound), and its product half stays owed as B7; the mirror's two residuals: a one-tick window where a held peer entry can land before a keystroke's change dispatches, and the record grain's last-writer-wins when a keystroke races a settle; the kahua stage's verdict door and its reader each run the Ed25519 signature equation (the door's double equation), so one check should serve both; the native WebCrypto Ed25519 speed lift (the daemon island's CPU burns in @noble/curves while verdicts memoize by exact bytes, true-only); the launcher's seat-law appendix tells every seat to name a stop in out.md, which a read seat cannot write (it hands back through its final message), so the read-seat brief line wants its own wording; two Train T pins from the 2026-11-04 test-surface reads (80 PRs grew the suite by 425 test files and 2,072 cases, 73% of the added cases in new files, with five consolidation targets; file-path-retired.test.ts outlived the one-commit rule, and two-vessel-mesh.test.ts runs in no project).
  • Owed, carried forward from the record batches (2026-10-31 to 2026-11-04; each reads as owed until a lane re-measures it against the tree):
    • After the founding: the comms channel for non-Claude seats (lane message tiddlers on the daemon wiki, operator 2026-11-04) rides #/the-order, rank 31. A Sol YIN reader over operator-daemon-behavior.ts and its onEa boot chain (1555 lines, 108 awaits), applying the house laws, then a YIN lane with five cures: (1) the working attachment resolves only the working slot; (2) authority readiness separates from maintenance, moving the floor migration, unbound-mint enumeration, refusal filing and additional-face boot to owned background stages; (3) every drain breathes, with a bounded batch per host turn and one lifecycle owner; (4) idempotency at the crash boundaries, so binding mint and lift rehome resume an owned durable op; (5) scopes derive and sovereignty stays. The ordered boot runs authority, archive standing, working-only attachment, pointer landed, verified dispatch, ea, then seat and lift, intents, extra faces, maintenance; the pointer wait is unbounded today.
    • Liveness (operator coffee-talk, 2026-11-03): fixed liveness bounds (the eight-second document bound on lift reads and flushes, the watchdog's breath silence) move toward breath-accrued suspicion (phi accrual from breath inter-arrival history at the FfzClock grain), per the clockless lease model. Safety stays clock-free. Landed markers say breath, never heartbeat.
    • The shared-holder tabs (operator, 2026-11-02, the page-owned keys' lane; the keys themselves burned, #/rulings 2026-11-12): a holder-protocol restart for SharedWorker shared-holder tabs (a holder-side swap broadcast to every tab), and the one-by-one fail-closed of those tabs on their own arrival, wait on whether shared-holder mode survives the browser vessel lift.
    • Promote and lift residuals: the CAS pin has no unpin, so promoted bytes (the operator's own, possibly private, code in cleartext in a CAS built for public genesis blobs) stay pinned after an unpromote, which weighs the sealed-CAS train up; the kāpae shadow cache has no size bound (name a ceiling of standing edges per face, and a raise past it refuses by name); the browser board watcher has no successful-shutdown hook; the lift-once guard keys on title, so two door instances racing, or another title in one proposal batch, can slip it (bind single use to the proposal digest across the batch); a behavioural test that rotates a root and reads every founding pin unchanged (only a source check stands); resolve-binding.ts and slot-doc-resolver.ts create docs with an unbounded whenReady (bound them with the defer-only pattern); a fresh destination doc's repo.flush sometimes never returns (find why in the storage adapter or flush path); when the rehome flush times out a fresh doc and its cap already exist (mint after the flush, or clean up the orphan); deterministic materialization (deterministic-doc.ts) catches every find failure as first boot and should tell a confirmed absence from a timeout; the rest door's gate wait has no deadline; the promote live probe proves a module ran by reading the daemon's unrouted-save log line, so before that warning quiets, the staged hearth needs a bag that routes the probe's titles; a lares CLI verb for island.restart and a walk over a held-open UDS session.
    • L4 security-read residuals: (LOW) a non-default same-origin genesis base with a foreign-origin descriptor reads cross and a no-relay non-default base boots uncompared, so require a descriptor on the page origin whenever the base differs from the default; bags//daemon/x with an empty segment reads as not control-plane (fold empty segments or refuse // in lift titles); $:/tags/RawMarkup|Global|Stylesheet, a charset'd js type and core or plugin title overrides still lift (operator-signed, fleet-reaching); a control that <root>/wikis/daemon/ stays absent; the island verb's ensure comes from its caller (idempotent, a note). The hand-only key or per-call gesture and the self-reported proposal taint with replay stand beside the lift tension. Hypothesis, never a claim: that a hand-only refusal string steers an injected AI; measure it with the sensorium self-measure before any canon vows it.
    • Harden residuals: genesis-floor.ts and meme-face.ts call the bare frame verdict and skip the smol cross-check (a TOML reject loses root fields and never gains an address).
    • Isomorphism: the browser vessel composes no recall, telemetry or worldline verb plane, which ride the shared daemon verb provider cap on node; name whether the browser composes them or they stay node-only by nature.
    • A far pattern integrity (operator, 2026-11-02): user-critical acts take a gesture page-realm code cannot reach, refined way down the road. The prior art rests in git (the promote branch's closed shadow root, isTrusted gate and one-shot claim, burned). The consent research measured that isTrusted cannot tell a DOM-only external driver from an in-page .click() (CDP, Playwright, WebDriver and OS input arrive trusted; extension content-script clicks arrive untrusted) and that a closed shadow root is no boundary. The lean: a host strip plus a private consent MessagePort registered at trusted bootstrap before promoted code runs, ordinary mirror ports proposing and never committing, a nonce receipt binding {act, wiki, digest, device, frontier}, activation never required; stronger boundaries are a separate-origin mirror iframe or an extension-owned window. It must never block an AI driving the browser.
    • Grammar, CI and tools: the weave passes a stored bcc through without recomputing, and the seal covers STX to ETX only, so the SOH head (to=, namespace) sits outside it (should the seal cover the head?); a sidebar-search autofocus when the mirror's full promotion lands may steal focus mid-fill and feed the draft-save flake; tsc -p packages/lararium-tw5 reports TS6059 rootDir errors from memetic-frame imports (the typecheck witness passes; find whether the witness invocation misses them); tools/land-train.test.mjs mints land-train-* temp dirs under tmpdir() and never removes them (an afterEach rmSync of every dir it mints); tools/crossing-witness.sh fails in lane worktrees because its docker build wants mempalace/README.md, which a provisioned worktree lacks; the CI venv cache key ignores the mempalace submodule commit and the Python patch version; CI wants bubblewrap and unprivileged user namespaces so the wall proofs in tools/commit-relay.test.mjs run instead of skip; the launcher reads true usage from rollout token_count events (seat-harness holds the guard), and a lead lifetime cap or fresh-per-step lead stands in the harness.
  • Mana-triad retirement (operator ruling, 2026-11-04): mana, manao and manaoio scalars retire house-wide, and no gate or federation authority derives from them.
  • Swarm controls at standing (operator ruling, 2026-11-04): wiki tiddlers that alter swarm control read valid only when the vessel or the hand signs them, and item 7 lands at standing before founding.
  • Red-first, homed (operator ruling, 2026-11-04): each fix lands a behavior red with its control in the narrowest existing home, and a new file opens only at a distinct runtime, boundary, harness or suite class.

Decisions ~ what shapes unbuilt work, and why

The hosted walk's own mechanics (the token, the grant, the spent-set, the hosting doc) read in lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/membership-doctrine#/the-invite. The decisions below constrain a slice still on the road.

  • One sorter, inside the gate, before the verdict. Every vessel socket opens through the sealed face gate's attach, verdict and open sequence; the gate sorts before the verdict, so posture reads once at admission and never at share time. GateSessionChannel opens only after the verdict (face-gate.ts, gate-session-channel.ts). One gate stands per (Nexus, route).
  • The knock. A gate answers an upgrade only on a path derived from its own key (gateKnock via knockSegment in packages/lararium-mesh/src/gate-bundle.ts), so a dialer holding no pin draws no HTTP 101 — the WireGuard mac1 shape. The path still rides the upgrade in the clear on plain ws; full client-proves-first waits for Noise IK (R-WIRE).
  • Silence, one deadline. No denial frame and no close codes: one drop deadline drawn at accept, so verify time leaks nothing and a PRIVATE stranger looks exactly like a failed proof.
  • The multi-Nexus fold. A gate answers strangers if ANY Nexus it stands in reads OPEN. One gate key serves every Nexus a vessel carries, so this rule reduces exactly to per-Nexus posture once each Nexus carries its own wire key (R-WIRE) — not an interim.
  • Carriage keys on the guest's PROVEN leaf. A hearth-issued stub would read as a bearer capability to another guest's record and as a hearth-minted cross-lineage linker. The leaf already proves on every socket, so keying on it adds no linkage the hearth lacks; a fresh leaf opens a fresh carriage (#/open, carriage across leaves).
  • One store door. A command that touches the store runs inside the standing vessel over the owner-only socket, else owns the store itself for that moment. A second Repo beside a live holder writes bytes the replica never sees. tools/direct-repo-witness.sh names the two lawful new Repo( sites and reads any other as DRIFT.
  • No hearth-local deny set. Kapae and the roll suffice; a deny set would make a seizable list.
  • No node carriage. A node serves as its own durable store and no story walks its carriage; ruling N holds as a sorter property and W as the hearth's service. Node carry would open a new user story, never owed by N or W.
  • Hosting stays on the worn persona leaf. ROOT-ON-FOUNDER keeps a persona root on one vessel, so one face cannot host from two stores unseen; the first-roll rail surfaces the remaining case. The face-switch act inherits it.
  • A cliff takes a funnel, then one assembled commit. Where one shape change crosses three packages, the fixtures funnel through one helper first; narrow builders each write one patch against a fixed contract; one lander proves them together in a scratch worktree and commits once, so the main tree never holds half a flip. Three cliffs stand on the road: the charter flip, the Nexus rename and the socket cutover.
  • The socket's handshake changes once. R-WIRE and the face proof build as ONE wire train: the signed admit, then the fleet-join door, then one socket cutover ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/face-proof ). The tree between those steps reads no earlier shape; it stands as a build order, never a compatibility mode.
  • A pure library names its caller. A module built ahead of its consumer stays out of the package barrel until the commit that consumes it. Until a production path calls it, a module stands specified in code, never built.
  • vessel found keeps a charterless road. The Stable founding act becomes the HEARTH's rite; a herm still founds as a place with no face, and a joiner still founds through an admit.
  • The carriage verifier holds the root: it checks the epoch, the signature AND a subject root it holds locally, and refuses by name without that root.
  • Recovery contact placement reads as advice the house names at enrolment, never a refusal. QR pairing shows a check string on both screens after the channel opens.
  • The floor vessel key drops its next-key commitment, inside the one identity train. It changes the vessel's signed inception bytes; the operator may overturn it.
  • A long-silent fleet device catches up before it acts: a presentation carries its presenter's frontier of the face's withdrawal history, and a gate that holds a later one answers unconfirmed and hands over what the device lacks. No clock; a house reading the operator may overturn ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/face-proof#/the-withdrawal-proof ).
  • The strangers' dial drops the NEWEST arrival when its slots fill; a slot frees by the operator's answer or a roll.
  • Every browser leaf counts as evictable: storage.persist() refuses in a plain tab, so no leaf holds the only copy of anything, and no passkey slot stands as the only way in ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/device-capabilities-2026 ).
  • A restarted always-on node serves no hosted phone until a human unlocks. The resolver and the terminator keep answering, so the page loads and the sync waits; the page says so in one line, until the ember lands.
  • A crossing gets recorded, never interior life. Only an authority, vessel, federation or externally meaningful crossing becomes distributed canon ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/api/mandate-activity-provenance ).
  • A package names no vessel. Every entity starts at the herm floor; a seated persona lights the Lararium stack over it. Only a lararium lights a Pronaos, and a herm mirrors a house's published bytes by CID ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/pronaos#/the-placement ).
  • Deferred by the house: realm feeding (two carriers disagree on max over per-writer slots against a blank merge; it waits for the first-realm arc), community exit, and funding, where payment may buy capacity and never standing.

The road ~ what stays unbuilt, in the order a builder walks it

The organizing law: identity bytes (the Nexus AID, the device edge, the dyad scope, the seats, the sentinel) change ONCE, inside one train. The owner law: each slice lands its logic in modules, and one named owner per step commits the one compose hunk in open-node-vessel.ts and open-browser-vessel.ts; every other lane stops at a module boundary. Each lane's first commit carries its canon. The fence (#/rulings) bounds the whole road: every slice that changes a shape lands before the second operator seats a key in the first Nexus.

The order: HEARTH → GROUND → SUBSTRATE → HARBOR → KAHU PLANE → ONE FACE → TWO HEARTHS, with train T's re-cut beside every path (#/the-order ranks the slots). The re-found's train rides inside it — R-ENGINE's fold and R-CUSTODY's seal in SUBSTRATE; R-CHARTER → R-WIRE, then R-KAHU, R-ENGINE's offers and R-EMBER, in the KAHU PLANE. Where the train's older order (the engine after the wire) and the six paths disagree, operator-ruled order governs: the engine fold and the custody seal land early, and the container's tail lands before the charter train.

0 · HEARTH ~ the memory-stage hearth (no identity bytes; leads the road; items in #/stride-0-hearth)

  • The walk: one machine, one operator. A node founds and stands at its floor, unlocks by the operator's hand, and serves its own page from its own origin; the page stands a browser vessel at its floor, binds a passphrase slot before any face secret, and joins the node's face through the fleet-join acts; a page edit reaches <projections>/wikis/daemon-<F>, a CLI write shows in the page, and the MCP seat reads and writes the same wiki ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/pronaos #/the-first-arrival; lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/session-contract #/the-hands). The harness walks it on a scratch root through the test door; the operator walks it on the hearth that holds the house's memory.
  • The stage: tags and one index view on the daemon wiki, never a new store or plane. A working stage holds what the controller's scratch files hold today; an episodic tier keeps scout reports, walks and incidents; survivors reach bags/ carriers by promotion under a person's ruling, and the fire of otakiage forgets what landed ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/docs/pono/otakiage ). No item reads a clock: an item's freshness reads as the commit or wave that last re-checked it, and a recurrence counts repeat incidents.
  • The seat: an agent writes the stage as a seat, never as a key; its writes land in the working layer, and the profile gate refuses it every authority-changing verb (F11).
  • The owner law holds here too: a hearth item reaches open-node-vessel.ts and open-browser-vessel.ts only through the named owner, and the shore's files (packages/lararium-web/**) take the hearth's page items as a shore lane.

1 · GROUND ~ the residue (no identity bytes; waits on nothing; rides beside SUBSTRATE on disjoint files)

  • The receipt ledger's direct leg: the three receipts of #/rulings stand where a CLI meets its vessel ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/receipt-ledger ). The leg across a herm rides THE WIRE (W12 of #/stride-1-spine).
  • The evergreen sweep and custody's edges, itemized as G6 (#/after-the-seat) and G7 (#/stride-1-spine).
  • Measures: the full-corpus per-verb flush re-measure; pack-PIN recursion; a production assertBodyIndexTier caller.
  • Canon direction the build owes: canon crosses hearth → leaf; a leaf never writes the hearth's canon documents, and edits land in the working layer. The admitted leaf-side mount flag stops mount-path writes, while Repo sync carries those documents in both directions.
  • A binding lands after its document rests: a slot mint records the binding only on the vessel store's acknowledgment (the receipt capability, receipt:request / receipt:issued); a mint no binding names stays listed (lares vessel unbound), and nothing removes one.

2 · SUBSTRATE ~ two SOLO relays, the fold first, then the seal

Each runs one spirit per commit, and every commit leaves HEAD building. No other node lane runs beside the seal: it owns the compose hunk.

R-ENGINE, the fold. * The R-ENGINE fold target dependency line, in fixed order: automerge-repo and its four listed adapters at alpha.5 → @automerge/automerge 3.4.1 → @keyhive/keyhive 0.3.0-alpha.1; no Subduction or Onomancy dependency → the genesis re-bake (GENESIS_ENGINE_CID, every fixture holding the hearth true-name, the untracked CAS in the same commit) → the fold's own live proof on the rebuilt image, with a pushed shard run reading green. The manifests hold all three dependency steps (packages/lararium-node/package.json, packages/lararium-keyhive/package.json); a Keyhive Archive and a cap event carry no format tag, so bytes one Keyhive version wrote refuse under another and a version move re-founds every vessel. The fold's own live proof on the rebuilt image stands owed. * TW5 core and the memetic-wikitext bootstrap fold into engineCid; the vocabulary leaves the engine as content; the coal-only seed carries no schemaVersion; the island schema_version drops its counter.

R-CUSTODY, the seal (P3-SEAL). * What stands sealed: the custody root and the identity-anchors family: the keyhive archive, each face's own veil archive (veil-archive-g<group digest>.bin on the node, keyed by its PersonaGroup; the browser's veil-archive-h<N> record still keys by handle index until K15 step 6), each face's anchors and the anchor roster. A founding seals them from its first write and hands out every keyhive Archive it stood before the provider leaves; the veil derives from the sealed custody root; a seat (custody-seat.ts) carries one holder per identity home, and each record reads whole or refuses by name, never empty. A creator that stands again restores from its Archive: cap events carry membership and never a creator's key agreement state. A stand mints one contact card and its Archive leaves after that mint, so the Archive lineage opens every prekey the identity ever presented. A browser vessel keeps the Archives its founding and its admit hand out in its keystore beside the device seed, in the clear, until browser custody wraps both (browser-keyhive-archive.ts). * Every writer family rests under its custody key: the carrier table ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/custody#/the-table ; vault-carriers.ts) names floor and floor-plain outside the vessel's keys, hot and cold under the vessel key, serving under SK, and outbox under an outbox key the vessel key wraps. SK mints at the founding; a human unlock opens it through the vessel key's entry in its serving tree (sk-slots, a bare floor record; serving-key.ts, custody-session.ts). Serving records and ember slots stay owed (E2); sealed outbox parcels stand, while ship and delete stay owed (K13). Each sealed record passes through the one sealed writer (sidecar → re-open → compare → rename, never sealing in place) and the sealed open. The per-face backup bundle stands built, cut and restore. * No keyring stands (operator ruling, #/rulings): the code holds no per-Nexus convergence secret. No custody record, seat mint, admission delivery or charter commitment carries one, and a persona admission's signed transcript names no keyring digest. A private body seals under its bag's content key, which reaches its readers through a lockbox ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/sealed-store#/keys ). A seat's act and a roll mint nothing beside the charter epoch. The same ruling's standing law binds every later slice: let a counter, wherever one appears, order one writer's own emissions only; keep the face at the session capability; keep every verified fork head visible; retain by causal evidence. * The handle-book: the recogniser's handle-book rests sealed under the S key. Nothing at its path reads as a book that recognises nobody; a record that stands and does not open, or opens to another shape, refuses by name at every read and every write, and a save lands only over nothing or over a snapshot that reads whole (packages/lararium-node/src/node-circle-store.ts). The follow graph holds one home, the persona-scoped circles plane the daemon's circle verbs write, and no custody record carries an edge. * The fleet-join family: one face's root-signed answer carries its device edge, signed gate carriage, island reach, sealed address secret, vessel face-key certificates and add-a-face grants. A join lands through one checked door. device-admit names the internal signed edge format, and cabal-join still names people and realm admission. * The vessel-identity family: the persona roster, the pet-name map, the declaration map and the published-face record rest sealed under the S key as hot records, and the worn face lives as session state and rests in no record (F9); each persona root rests as a cold record, opened for one act; the floor vessel key rests beside its signed inception, every load proves that inception, and no next-key seed or commitment rides it. The roster commits a root: a mint writes the root record, then the roster line that carries the root's public key, so a reading of a public key answers off the hot roster and opens no root. A root record with no roster line reads as an orphan: no reader holds it, lares persona list names it, and the same mint run again adopts it. A roster line whose root record went missing, or names another key, refuses by name at the act that needs the seed, and nothing mints over it. A hot record with nothing at its path reads as holding nothing; a record that stands and does not open, or opens to another shape, refuses by name at every read and every write. A vessel key mints only inside a held custody, and lands with its inception under the custody's one claim: the key file commits the mint, so two acts never commit two keys, and a boot loads a key and never mints one. The floor reads no record of the family, so a carried-Nexus reading by persona (a seated chair, a kept consent) answers empty until the key arrives, and a sovereign composition reads the roster whole before it stands (packages/lararium-node/src/node-vessel-identity.ts). A floor composition reads no roster, so it shuts every fleet lane: it sorts every device as set aside, sets a proven sibling aside, and its fetch door reads no peer as fleet, the pinned join gate's key included (packages/lararium-node/src/floor-fleet-lanes.ts). It still folds the one fleet board its own device edge names in the clear, so the Binding Gate reads this vessel's own seat at every standing. * The device recovery share: each face's device share rests as one COLD record under the S key, opened for the act that mints it and the quorum that assembles it (packages/lararium-node/src/recovery-share-store.ts). Nothing at a face's path reads as none landed; a record that stands and does not open, or opens to another shape, refuses the read and the write by name, so a wrong key replaces no share and no split lands over one it could not read. A share lands once per generation: a second split of the same generation refuses, since it would orphan the off-device shares a person already holds. A split hands the person both off-device shares before the device share rests, so a run that stops between the two leaves nothing sealed and the same command splits afresh. * The walk records: each hearth a vessel walks at keeps one HOT record sealed under the S key, named by a digest of that hearth's gate key (packages/lararium-node/src/node-walk-store.ts). A record holds bearer invites, the hearth's grant and one receipt per carried document ({ cid: "blake3:<hex>", ref, key }, whose key opens that document), so it counts as a secret record. The record rests in the storage home of the custody seat: the store takes no directory, so no caller files it beside a document store the vessel key never spans. Nothing at a hearth's path reads as no walk there; a record that stands and does not open, opens to another shape, or rests filed under another hearth's name refuses the read, the list and the update by name, and an update seals a whole record or nothing. Each change reads, decides and seals under one custody holder, so one invite leaves a wallet once. A floor composition reads no walk record and presents no walk; a fresh browser device stands at the floor under the floor card it carries in at the page's carriage door, and a joined face crosses under its root-signed pin. A key that locks under a live walk costs the walk one message, which it logs. lares walk, walk take and walk invite ride the custody door (walk.state · walk.take · walk.invite): a carried invite as a verb's argument, or an invite as a verb's outcome, would rest in the daemon's document in the clear. * The hosting records: a hearth's hosting state, each spend and its walkers' carriage rest as HOT records sealed under the S key, in the storage home of the custody seat (packages/lararium-node/src/hosting-store.ts, hosting-carry.ts). The sealed writer writes a whole file and appends to none, so each spend lands as ONE record of its own, named by a digest of its nonce or marker: the record's presence IS the burn, and it reads, decides and seals under one custody holder. The spend record commits a redemption, and what rides ahead of it re-derives: the grant from the epoch, the leaf, the nonce and the claim, so a crash before the answer replays as retry; the operator's counts from the records standing, by name. A roll's state record commits the roll, and the hosting doc re-derives from it: the next roll and each sovereign stand land an act the sealed state holds and the doc lacks (landHostingActs). The sweep of dead epochs' records passes through the seat's removal door (removeCustodyRecord), which removes only a record this vessel's key opens; a spend that sealed after a roll retired its epoch burns nothing live and leaves with the next sweep. Each carried value rests as one record whose name carries its address and its length, so a guest's bytes read off its records and no counter stands beside them. One process serves a hearth's carriage (the standing vessel's own gate, under its store claim), and its contacts and deposits run in turn, so a quota reads the bytes the last deposit left and a reclaim never discards on a reading a contact moved meanwhile. A record that stands and does not open refuses by name: a burn nobody can read never reads as unspent, and a carried value nobody can open never reads as absent. A floor composition reads no hosting record, so a locked hearth sorts no socket as a walker. lares host, host roll and host invite ride the custody door (host.state · host.roll · host.invite): a minted invite as a verb's outcome would rest in the daemon's document in the clear. * The seal ceremony: the growth rite's two records rest HOT under the S key in the seal home (packages/lararium-node/src/seal-ceremony.ts): transition-pending.json prepares a crossing, and transitions.json commits its record. A seal verifies the crossing whole, appends its record (the commit point), then removes the pending record, only while that record still holds the crossing it sealed; a seal that committed and stopped resumes to one kept record and no pending record, and kept records with no pending record read clean. A chair's own seat key signs for the ceremony behind the custody door (seat-key-store.ts), and only what the standing state licenses: seal.sign names a seat by its public key and signs a charter roll that steps from the standing head, reveals at least that head's count of the seats it committed one digest each, seats only keys this vessel holds and re-arms every seat it seats, where the charter's lineage verifies and its head binds the seated roster; it refuses every other image however well formed, and a persona root holds no seat record, so it signs no roll; the rite's hands mark through crossing.open · bind · sign · witness · seal, which take no image and sign the crossing that stands open (a face's seat keys sign; a witness's root attests). The rite arms itself behind the same door (seat.arm-genesis, seat.arm-roll): each standing chair's seat key mints there, each chair's NEXT key prints once as a cold-key card the operator reads back before the charter moves (a staged test root alone stands in), a roll adopts each card's key as its chair's seat key, and a superseded seat key retires once no held charter seats it and no open crossing names it (seat.retire). An earlier charter shape reads by name (nexusCharterShape) and licenses nothing. No command file writes a custody record: a census derives every sealed record's name, every write door and every writer function from the carrier table and the custody modules, and reads red on a command source that calls a door, names a writer function, or names a record beside a file-system write of its own (packages/lares-cli/tests/laws/custody-stays-behind-the-door.law.test.ts). * The face proof's records: face-proof-key rests COLD, one face's proof key at one Nexus, held by the root-holding vessel alone, its file named for the face so that face's backup bundle carries it; vessel-face-key rests HOT, this vessel's own face-scoped key, and stays on its vessel, outside every bundle. Fleet join calls the certificate and grant path, and the socket cutover (train W) carries the removal and gate session paths. Two doors on the custody seat sign and return (signAsFace, signAsVesselFace): each signs exactly the image handed in under the key its scope names and hands out no secret, and a store never lands over another standing key (packages/lararium-node/src/face-key-custody.ts; the library names the shapes in FACE_PROOF_CUSTODY_RECORDS). * The gate's two root doors (the node composer seats both, open-node-vessel.ts): a floor gate's bundle derives through the floor key's door, which answers at a locked vessel, hands back a fresh child and never the floor key (vesselFloorKeyDoor, packages/lararium-node/src/node-vessel-identity.ts); a sovereign gate's bundle derives through the custody seat's door, which answers only while the seat holds its key (custodyGateDoor, packages/lararium-node/src/custody-seat.ts). The doors carry two types, so no line swaps them by accident; a root refuses the other tier's door by name; each seat's door refuses the other tier's child; and a weld holds the two mints to the two seats ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/face-proof#/the-gate-exchange ). * The gate's memory records (the vessel's gates read and write them, vessel-gates.ts): a sovereign gate's withdrawal memory rests one record per face key in the vessel's storage home, named by the face's proof-subject digest and sealed under the S key, so a locked vessel's disk names no face key (gate-memory in the carrier table; packages/lararium-node/src/gate-memory-records.ts). Each write reads, decides and seals under one custody holder, and refuses where it would drop an event the record holds; a locked seat refuses by name. An older copy of a record restored by hand makes a gate forget until the next honest vessel dials, and no fence stands against that hand. A record that stands and does not fold whole refuses by name and reports it (GateMemoryRecordTorn): the asking gate falls silent, no keep writes over the record, and a person repairs it by hand, where a record read as nothing would let a fresh memory admit a vessel the record once denied. No backup bundle carries the record: a vessel that stands again knows nothing ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/face-proof#/the-withdrawal-proof ). * The session and the boot: the daemon stands at the floor holding no S key and unlocks on a custody frame of its owner-only socket (unlock · lock · custody · act · backup, packages/lararium-node/src/custody-frames.ts), handled ahead of every verb, so the credential the CLI reads on its TTY reaches no receipt, outcome or log. One holder keeps the opened S key in node-main (custody-holder.ts); an unlock tears the standing composition down and stands a fresh one on the same floor (open-node-vessel.ts), a lock returns it to the floor, and three failed unlocks spend a boot's attempts. A composition that refuses above the floor leaves the floor standing and names its fault once. The settle sweeps a sidecar whose write never landed. The test door (tools/unlock-test-custody.mjs) opens marked scratch roots alone, and the marker earns one loud line in vault status and the boot log. Every boot composes at the floor: nothing stored lifts it. A daemon island holds no S key, so its archive exports cross the custody write door (custody:write) and the main thread seals them. Every broad sync port, the daemon island's and each wiki island's, crosses one hardened facade on both ends: an arrival that reads as no sync message drops at the port and never reaches a listener that would throw on it (packages/lararium-mesh/src/island-repo.ts). The daemon island's gates ask the main thread what a device's edge reads on its fleet's board through the dyad capability (dyad:ask), which answers aside wherever it cannot read. Each fold of the fleet's edge board sets off the key side's settle on an unlocked vessel, with no timer and no person: the main thread places the island's seat-duties verb, awaits its answer and keeps the signed rolls (packages/lararium-node/src/seat-settle.ts; lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/kapae#/what-the-code-holds ). Those two capabilities and the receipt capability answer the daemon island's channel alone: each worker's class binds at its spawn (packages/lararium-node/src/worker-handle.ts), a wiki island reaches the cas capability alone while its custody write and its receipt ask refuse by name and its dyad ask draws aside, and a session island reaches no vessel capability. A command that acts with no vessel standing opens its own key session at the first sealed record it touches (packages/lares-cli/src/offline-custody.ts): a terminal prompt, and under a marked scratch root the published test route. A sealed record's act rides an act frame of the custody door and never a store verb: one typed table bounds the acts (packages/lararium-node/src/sealed-acts.ts), a standing vessel answers with no second prompt, and beside a standing vessel a command opens no key session of its own, while a root's recovery split refuses there (packages/lares-cli/src/sealed-act-door.ts). The fleet mirror of a pet-name and a declared Handle rides that door too: selves.carry names a face and which name, the vessel's main thread reads the name off its sealed record and writes it onto that face's own persona plane and no other face's, only where that plane's rest seals (a clear rest refuses the carry and the name stays on its record), lares persona sync fills and never replaces, and no verb takes or answers a face's own name ( lar:///ha.ka.ba/lares/api/pono/waking-floor#/the-shape ). * The per-face backup bundle, its cut: lares vault backup --face <N> --out <file> cuts one face's bundle through the standing vessel's custody door, under the law at lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/custody#/the-cut . The carrier table's bundle column picks the rows, the frame asks the vessel's own route again, the daemon island seals the memory section to the pair the bundle key derives, and the command binds the bundle's routes in its own process (packages/lararium-mesh/src/face-bundle.ts; packages/lararium-node/src/face-bundle-cut.ts, custody-frames.ts; packages/lares-cli/src/vault-backup.ts). The handle-book tags each record with the face that recognised it, so a bundle carries its own face's records alone. A node vessel's daemon island stands its content-key holder off the document it owns; no content key mints on a production vessel until a session signer stands (U1), so a cut taken before one stands carries an empty memory section. * The per-face backup bundle, its restore: lares vault restore <file> brings a face back onto a fresh vessel as a recovery act, under the law at lar:///ha.ka.ba/lararium/mesh/custody#/the-restore . The vessel checks the root against the newest key history it reads, seals one commit record, lands the carried records additively, and lights the face through the face rite: a new seat under the same fixed prefix (packages/lararium-node/src/face-bundle-restore.ts; the act table's bundle.restore and bundle.import rows; packages/lares-cli/src/vault-restore.ts).
